A closed sample at n = 10, one runner, one boot, re-derivable from that run's `seccomp-variance.latest.json` artifact. `microseconds_per_notification`, median [min–max], range, and pairwise-gap median/max over 45 pairs: `path_heavy` 62.70 [59.42–68.32] 14.2% 6.0%/13.9%; `reference_app_api` 65.62 [64.44–74.47] 15.3% 2.3%/14.4%; `shell_heavy` 71.06 [67.06–76.09] 12.7% 4.6%/12.6%; `reference_app_socket` 72.44 [65.87–86.17] 28.0% 7.4%/26.7%; `compute_only` 24.79 [4.04–131.73] 515% 127%/188% at 7 of 10 rated.

Raising `REPEATS` shrinks the smaller component and leaves the larger untouched, so five-fold wall clock buys a few percent of the wrong number. **This does not contradict the earlier refusal — it supplies the evidence that refusal said it lacked**, which was that more samples inside one run cannot reach a whole-run effect. `notifications_observed` was a *single* value across all ten draws for every arm (2078 / 827 / 372 / 833 / 78), so the entire within-run spread is in the timing numerator and the denominator repair is ruled out on CI as well as locally.

Three closed samples at n = 10, three boots, none folded into another. Within-run pairwise-gap medians across the three: `path_heavy` **6.0**/3.3/3.1%, `reference_app_api` 2.3/4.5/3.8%, `shell_heavy` 4.6/7.0/6.8%, `reference_app_socket` 7.4/7.8/10.1%, `compute_only` 127/98/70% at **7**, 8 and 6 of 10 rated. ~~`path_heavy` 2.3/3.3/3.1%~~ ~~at 10, 8 and 6 of 10 rated~~ **— two cells corrected 2026-08-10 by re-derivation from the artifacts. `path_heavy`'s first column carried `reference_app_api`'s 2.3% while the bullet above states `path_heavy` 6.0%/13.9% for the same run, and the control's first rated count read 10 where the same bullet states 7 of 10 with 3 of 10 non-positive. Both cells had propagated from the battery's own note, corrected there in the same pass. Neither reaches a conclusion: the load-bearing range `2.3–7.4%` in the bullet above is taken over all four arms and 2.3% is `reference_app_api`'s, so it stands.** **TIGHT-on-the-four and WIDE-on-the-control is therefore reproducible three for three, and that is what `REPEATS` is decided on.** The medians are not reproducible: `path_heavy` reads 62.70, 40.82, 43.97 µs/notification, and the across-run gaps between the three medians-of-ten are **42.3 / 46.3 / 47.7 / 59.1%** on the load-bearing arms and 140.5% on the control. Each of those points already aggregates fifty samples, so **fifty samples still leaves a 42–59% across-run gap** — the residual is not a sample-count problem at any `REPEATS` this battery could afford, which is the conclusion above in its strongest available form.
    - ⚠️ **CORRECTED BY THAT REPEAT: the bound reported in the next bullet does not survive it, and the next bullet is left standing with this correction attached rather than rewritten, because the figure it names was published to the owner and a silently improved number would hide that it moved.** The control's positive extreme — the defining quantity of any magnitude bound — reads **+0.010275**, **+0.017368** and **+0.029317 s** across the three samples, a **2.9× move** under identical construction on one runner class. In `31416959913` that extreme sits **above `shell_heavy`'s own overhead for the same run** (0.0188–0.0228 s), so a bound read off that sample's control would withhold a load-bearing arm outright. **There is no single number here to serve as a floor.** The measurement therefore *vindicates* the original refusal to install a magnitude bound rather than supplying what the refusal lacked, and the owner's decision needs no new evidence to stay where it is.
    - **What a magnitude bound built on this floor would look like, reported and NOT installed.** The sign test is `overhead_seconds <= 0`, so a magnitude bound would be `overhead_seconds <= FLOOR`. The control is the arm that measures the floor, and across one boot its overhead straddles zero: **−0.004659 s to +0.010275 s**, non-positive in 3 of 10, rated draws swinging **32.6×**. A bound read off it would be **FLOOR ≈ +0.0103 s** (its positive extreme) or **≈ 0.0149 s** if the peak-to-peak band is taken instead. **`n = 10`, one runner, one boot, one commit** — that is the n to read it against. Two consequences the owner should weigh rather than inherit: at 0.0103 s the control becomes withheld in **10 of 10** draws, which is defensible for a control but is a behaviour change and not a filter; and `shell_heavy`'s overhead is **0.0249–0.0283 s**, only **2.4×** the bound, so a bound set from the peak-to-peak band or nudged upward later would begin suppressing a load-bearing arm. **A third caveat is the one that matters most: this floor is a *within-run* figure, and the across-run spread is larger, so a bound built on it is too tight for any comparison made across runs.** Installing one requires deleting `test_a_small_positive_overhead_is_still_published_because_no_floor_is_known`, which is the intended cost, and the decision is the owner's.
  - ⚠️ **The "one anomalous run" reading is FALSIFIED at n = 11, and the arithmetic it was drawn from remains correct for the runs it describes.** The battery note concludes that T101's CI spread *"is one anomalous run and not a wide runner class"*, naming run 31403771772. Every CI run since 31400931286 publishes this figure as an artifact, so the sample is 11 runs on one runner class (`6.17.0-1020-azure` x86_64, 4 vCPU, CPython 3.12.13) rather than the three to five those readings were taken over. Recomputed from the artifacts with the note's own statistic — `|a − b| / mean(a, b)` over the four load-bearing arms, 45 pairs per arm, **180 pairs**:
    - **Excluding 31403771772 (n = 10): median 18.5%, max 69.6%.** The concordant runs do *not* agree to within 18.5% per arm; 18.5% is the **median** of that band and not its extent. The extreme pair is `path_heavy` 34.33 against 64.41 — runs 31404642569 and 31411573174, **neither of which is the run called anomalous**.
    - **Pairs involving 31403771772 span 0.1% – 43.6%**, so its maximum sits *below* the concordant maximum and its minimum is the smallest gap in the whole sample: on `path_heavy` it is 45.46 against 31407296884's 45.49, a 0.1% difference. **It is not separable from the class it was excluded from.**
    - **So the spread is wide across runs with no outlier**, which is a third reading and not a return to the struck between-runner one. The earlier bands — 1.2–13.8% at n = 3, 1.2–18.5% at n = 5 — are correct for their samples and did not generalise, which is the closed-sample lesson this corpus already records twice. **This is itself a closed sample at n = 11**, taken 2026-08-10; it is re-derivable rather than transcribed, by `gh run download <id> -n pytest-outcomes-and-native-overhead` over those runs and the formula above.
    - **What it changes for `REPEATS` is the comparator and not yet the constant.** A wide across-run spread with no outlier makes the within-run floor *more* decisive, not less: if k medians-of-5 come out tight against a 69.6% across-run maximum, the residual is demonstrably at the whole-run level and raising `REPEATS` cannot reach it. `REPEATS` therefore stays 5 on this pass, and the probe above is what can move it.
    - **The control's sign is common rather than diagnostic**, which strengthens the note's own struck sensitivity claim: `compute_only` came out non-positive in **8 of 11** CI runs. Its three published positive rates are **188.02, 62.74 and 74.04** µs/notification — a **3.0× swing** on one runner class, every value positive and every one passing the sign test, which is the clearest statement of why a one-sided detector is not a floor.
  - ⚠️ **WHAT CLOSING T101 REQUIRES, STATED AS A SET AND RE-VERIFIED RATHER THAN INHERITED — 2026-08-10. Four blockers, all four confirmed, one of them strictly worse than it had been recorded as, and the set did not grow.** This is a statement of the conditions, not a route to them and not an estimate of one. Each item below was checked against the artifact or the file it is about in this pass.
    - **1 — CI is structurally incapable of recording, confirmed unchanged.** `F2A_RECORD_MEASUREMENTS` occurs **once** in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, at line 514, and that line is a comment; it occurs in no other workflow and in no shell script in the repository. `record_filename` therefore selects `seccomp-overhead.latest.json` on every CI run that has ever happened, so no CI run can promote its figure to the recorded one. **The consequence is narrower than "CI takes no figure" and that distinction is the useful half**: every run since 31400931286 publishes a five-arm figure as an artifact, and three of them publish a k=10 within-run sample as well. What no run produces is a *recorded* measurement. The bullet above beginning "STILL OUTSTANDING" says no figure for either reference-application arm has been taken; that was true when written and is **superseded** — figures for both exist in the published artifacts, and what is absent is the recording, not the measurement.
    - **2 — No floor exists, and this is measured rather than merely absent, which is a strengthening of the old blocker and not a repeat of it.** The quantity that would define a magnitude bound is the control's positive extreme, and across three samples of identical construction on one runner class it reads **+0.010275, +0.017368 and +0.029317 s** — a **2.9× move**, re-derived from the artifacts in this pass. In `31416959913` it sits **above `shell_heavy`'s own overhead for that run** (0.0188–0.0228 s), so a bound taken from that sample would withhold a load-bearing arm outright. There is no single number here to be a floor, and the refusal to install one is what the measurement vindicates.
    - **3 — The across-run spread is wide with no outlier, so no single CI run's median is a decidable basis for Q-09.** Falsified at n=11 in the bullets above; the "one anomalous run" reading and the control-sign flag are both dead. **Spot-verified rather than re-litigated in this pass**: the named extreme pair reproduces exactly off the two runs' own artifacts — `path_heavy` **34.33** on 31404642569 against **64.41** on 31411573174, a gap of **60.9%** under the note's own statistic, neither run being the one once called anomalous. The **69.6%** maximum is over 180 pairs and was not re-derived here.
    - **4 — The committed artifact is unusable, and for three reasons rather than the one recorded.** `tests/batteries/results/seccomp-overhead.json` is the 2026-08-03 `6.12.76-linuxkit` aarch64 record. Its `environment` block carries **no `euid` key** — absent, not null, so the record cannot state its own privilege posture, which is the reason already on the record. Two further reasons were read off the same file in this pass and are strictly stronger. It holds **three arms** — `compute_only`, `path_heavy`, `shell_heavy` — so it carries **no figure for either reference-application arm**, and the reference application is T101's own outstanding clause; a record that cannot speak to the clause cannot discharge it whatever its privilege posture. And its `what_this_is_a_property_of[0]` is still the **hardcoded constant** naming Docker Desktop's linuxkit VM — the exact sentence `tests/removal_proofs.sh` plants as a defect to prove the reading that replaced it. **The artifact is not hand-edited**, on the ground already recorded twice above: the repair for a stale measurement is a new measurement, and editing a three-arm record's caveats would make it read as though someone had looked at it since.
    - **The set did not grow, because the socket arm is not a defect.** `reference_app_socket` is reproducibly the widest of the four load-bearing arms, and that was investigated in this pass as a candidate fifth blocker on the ground that every T101 figure rests on the four. It is **a property of the workload**: its denominator is fixed (`notifications_observed` at one distinct value across all ten draws in every arm in all three samples, **833 / 835 / 835** for this arm), and its `overhead_seconds` is only **4.9–7.7%** of its own `baseline_seconds` against 52–729% for the other three, so it differences two large medians to recover a small one and its width is the workload's own timing noise. The full derivation, including a repair planted in both directions that removes real dead time and **does not** narrow the width, is at the site in `tests/batteries/test_seccomp_overhead.py`. **No fix is claimed and none is installed.** ✅ **Confirmed prospectively by a fourth sample the claim did not have when it was written** — run `31421757875`, the CI run of the commit that recorded it: socket widest of the four again at 7.8% against 6.2%, denominator one distinct value in every arm again, taking that check to **20 of 20 arm-sample cells**, and overhead-over-baseline 6.4% against 459/56/41%.
    - **What the four blockers together require is an owner decision that is not taken here: which environment produces T101's authoritative figure.** The constraint is that no environment currently available satisfies all of the conditions at once. CI measures all five arms on a stable runner class at euid 0 and **cannot record**, and its across-run spread is wide with no outlier so one run's median does not decide. A privileged linuxkit container **can** record and is a different kernel and architecture, and this file's own standing rule is that two records taken on different kernels are not a before and an after and must not be subtracted — so a linuxkit recording does not inherit CI's evidence and CI's evidence does not license a linuxkit figure. ~~**The decision is left unmade.**~~ It is not a scheduling question and no route is invented for it here: whoever owns Q-09 chooses the environment whose posture the recorded figure is allowed to be a property of, and the recording follows that choice rather than preceding it.
    - ✅ **DECIDED 2026-08-10 as [OD-30](../001-discovery-validation/plan.md) — CI is the authoritative environment, the figure is published per arm against the same run's control with pooling forbidden, and one of the three limbs was declined on inspection.** The entry records the environment selection **comparatively** and refuses the deployment-representative reading: CI is a 4 vCPU hosted runner and the grounds that carry it are the eleven-run sample on one documented harness and the fact that `6.12.76-linuxkit`/`aarch64` is a kernel and architecture nothing deploys to. **The ground that CI is the only environment measuring all five arms is false and is struck at the entry**: `tests/batteries/results/seccomp-overhead.latest.json` on the linuxkit host is a five-arm record at `euid 0` carrying the `euid` key this task's blocker 4 records the committed artifact as lacking, so both environments measure five arms and only the linuxkit one can record. **Blocker 1 is narrowed rather than retired** — the line-514 comment reading is re-confirmed unchanged, and what the entry establishes is that the flag was never the obstacle, because `record_filename` selects a *filename* and CI's checkout is destroyed either way. The `workflow_dispatch` recording route is **declined, not deferred**: the artifact upload and the step-summary renderer already exist, so recording already costs the two deliberate human acts it was meant to buy, and setting the flag in CI would leave `seccomp-overhead.latest.json` unwritten, take the renderer's `NOT PRODUCED` branch, and fail the job while reporting that the battery probably skipped.
    - ⚠️ **What OD-30 does to this task's own criterion, stated because it is a narrowing and not a closure.** T101 asks for *"the figure and the shell-heavy arm that stresses it"* — two named subjects, so the entry's prohibition on pooling contradicts nothing here and a pooled workload-level figure was never this task's criterion. What the per-arm rule does put at risk is the other named subject. **The arm that sits inside the control's excursion is `shell_heavy`, not `reference_app_socket`**, and the two quantities are different: the socket arm is worst on *resolution* at 4.9–7.7% of its own baseline, while the rule tests *absolute* overhead against the control's absolute excursion, where the socket arm recovers ~0.05 s and ~~clears the control's widest observed +0.029317 s~~ **clears its own run's control in each of the three samples, by factors of 5.34, 1.83 and 1.23** *(the struck clause spliced two runs — ~0.05 s is `31415736559`'s socket figure and +0.029317 s is `31416959913`'s control extreme, and within that run the socket arm reads 0.036025–0.049227 s; corrected 2026-08-10)*, and `shell_heavy`'s 0.0188–0.0228 s in run `31416959913` does not. That is the hazard the magnitude-bound bullet below already names at a factor of 2.4. ~~**So T101 owes a figure on `path_heavy` and `reference_app_api`, and a bound on whichever arm the run's own control swallows**~~ **Superseded 2026-08-10 by limb ③'s revision — T101 owes a figure on all four load-bearing arms, each with the control ranges beside it, and no arm is withheld; see the bullet below** — and **Q-09 is unaffected**, its operative clause being a prohibitive-or-not decision rule that an overhead beneath the instrument's floor answers in the not-prohibitive direction. The criterion is not reworded to fit the ruling.
    - ⚠️ **LIMB ③ IS REVISED 2026-08-10 — NOTHING IS WITHHELD, AND WHAT THIS TASK OWES IS FOUR FIGURES RATHER THAN THREE AND A BOUND.** Every arm publishes its `overhead_seconds` with the same run's observed control excursion and the pooled control range beside it, and any overlap with either is stated **as an overlap on the same line as the figure it qualifies**. The revision is recorded as a **revision** rather than a narrowing or a correction: it is the same question revisited, the withholding clause is struck in place at the register, and the wrong-arm prediction stays readable because it is the evidence. **The defect that carried it is the comparator, not the arm**: a per-run maximum makes the published set a property of the draw, which is the reading blocker 3 above already forecloses — *"no single CI run's median is a decidable basis for Q-09"* — applied to the quantity the magnitude-bound bullet says there is *"no single number here to serve as a floor"* for.
    - ⚠️ **THE FINDING THIS SURFACED, AND IT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE RULE: `shell_heavy`'s OVERHEAD SITS AT THE SAME ABSOLUTE SCALE AS THE CONTROL'S NOISE, AND CLEARS ITS OWN RUN'S CONTROL ON ONE OF THE THREE RECORDED SAMPLES.** Re-derived from the three `seccomp-variance.latest.json` artifacts at 10 draws per arm, `overhead_seconds` against the same run's control positive extreme: `31415736559` +0.010275 s against 0.024945–0.028304 s, **10 of 10** draws above; `31416789165` +0.017368 s against 0.014794–0.017791 s, **1 of 10**; `31416959913` +0.029317 s against 0.018794–0.022802 s, **0 of 10**. Pooled over all 30 draws the control runs **−0.007711 to +0.029317 s**, non-positive in 9, and `shell_heavy` is **the only one of the four load-bearing arms that overlaps it at all** — while `path_heavy`, `reference_app_api` and `reference_app_socket` clear it on every draw at minima of 0.081526, 0.032178 and 0.031767 s. **This is a fact about Q-09's own named arm and it is stated where the figure is met** rather than behind a withhold rule: the entry that struck the arm's number would have hidden it. The struck rule's reach was also wider than the register recorded — it withholds this arm on **two** of the three samples, not on the one the entry named. ✅ **Confirmed prospectively by a fourth sample the claim did not have when it was written** — run `31427947131`, the CI run of the commit that recorded it: control −0.012760 to +0.021070 s, `shell_heavy` 0.024417–0.028696 s above that extreme in **10 of 10** draws, so the arm clears here and clears by **1.16**, the narrowest margin of the four samples. The count becomes **two of four published and two of four withheld** under the struck rule, and pooled over all **40** draws the control runs −0.012760 to +0.029317 s, non-positive in 14, with `shell_heavy` still the only one of the four that overlaps it.
    - ⚠️ **THE OBJECTION THAT SURVIVES, AND THE ELEMENT IT ADDED.** Disclosure beside a figure lets a reader lift the figure and leave the disclosure, which is the failure withholding exists to prevent and which this corpus has already paid for: `dry-run-verdict`'s motivating artifact disclosed itself with a banner and a directory name and its decision row still read `verifier is a headline feature`. The remedy taken is the one already adopted there — **line-locality**, not withholding — so the overlap statement is required on the figure's own line. ~~**The machine-readable half is owed and not installed**: a per-arm field asserting whether the arm cleared its own run's control would carry the mark into anything reading the artifact, and adding one is code OD-30 does not authorise against a measurement nobody has taken.~~ ✅ **INSTALLED 2026-08-10, and the measurement it was said to lack was taken in the same pass.** `overhead_against_this_runs_control` and `overhead_against_this_runs_control_because` sit beside every arm's `overhead_seconds`, produced by the fixture rather than appended to a file — which `test_this_runs_measurement_reached_the_file_it_was_asked_for` requires, since it asserts `json.loads(path.read_text()) == measurement` and a field written anywhere but the measurement path fails there correctly.
      - ⚠️➡️✅ **THE FIELD SHIPPED WITH THE DEFECT IT WAS BUILT TO CLOSE, ONE LEVEL DOWN, AND IT IS REPAIRED 2026-08-10.** Run `31435892323`'s record published `shell_heavy` as `overhead_against_this_runs_control: "clears-this-runs-control"` while `..._because` one key away said the margin was *"OVERLAPPING the control's own draw excursion ... so this run's own control draws roamed far enough to have produced a difference this size"*. Both statements were true and only one was machine-readable, so **a consumer filtering arms on `== "clears-this-runs-control"` collected that arm and never reached the qualification** — the verdict had become the new bare figure.
      - **The repair is a nested object and deliberately not a sibling key.** `overhead_against_this_runs_control` is now `{verdict, qualified_by, qualified_by_means}`. A sibling field would have been readable without prose and would have failed the other half of the requirement: the naive filter still succeeds and still reads clean, so nothing stops the verdict travelling alone. Nesting makes that comparison **false for every arm**, so a consumer who does not destructure gets an empty result — visibly broken — rather than a qualified arm wearing a clean answer. **The alternative loses on the failure mode, not on taste.**
      - **The overlap does not decide the verdict and the separation is held in the signatures.** `excursion_qualification` is not given the control's overhead, so it cannot become a comparator whatever a later edit does to its body; `control_clearance` is unchanged. Folding the overlap into the verdict is `58a6277`'s defect, which run `31434583620` made concrete and which `test_the_verdict_compares_like_with_like_and_the_excursion_never_decides` refuses. `qualified_by` has **four** readings and not a boolean, on the T206 ground the verdict has four: the control has no excursion to be qualified against, and an arm with no overhead has nothing for a range to qualify.
      - **The committed record was migrated by re-derivation, and it is the emitter that governs.** The CI artifact's derived fields were recomputed through the module's own `control_clearance` and `excursion_qualification` from readings it already carried, under an assertion that every verdict and every sentence came back **byte-identical**; no measured value moved. Both directions plant inside that committed record — `shell_heavy` reads `overlaps-this-runs-control-excursion` and the other three read `stands-clear-of-this-runs-control-excursion` — and `test_a_committed_record_plants_both_qualifications` is what notices if that collapses. **Six removal proofs** now cover the clearance machinery, which had none.
    - **It is four readings and not a boolean, on the T206 ground.** `clears-this-runs-control`, `does-not-clear-this-runs-control`, `is-this-runs-control` and `no-overhead-to-clear-with`. A boolean maps three of those onto one `false` — the control compared with itself, an arm with no overhead to clear anything with, and an arm the control swallows — which reads as *the mechanism was measured and found not to clear* when only the third says that. `CLEARANCE` carries the prose for each in the shape `UNRATED` beside it already uses, and the set is closed in both directions by `test_every_clearance_reading_is_recorded_in_prose`.
    - ⚠️ **THE COMPARATOR WAS WRONG FOR ONE COMMIT AND THE RUN THAT COULD FALSIFY IT DID.** It shipped in `58a6277` testing each arm against the control's *draw excursion* — the range of the control's raw pairwise differences. Run `31434583620` returned **three of the four load-bearing arms as not clearing**, while that same run's k=10 probe puts all four clear on **10 of 10** draws. The defect is a statistic mismatch that runs one way: every arm publishes a difference of two medians, the excursion is a wider quantity, and testing the first against the second under-claims. Corrected in `cc34adb` to the like-for-like comparison — the arm's difference of medians against the control's difference of medians — with the excursion kept as the **second** range limb ③ names, stated as an overlap on the figure's own line where one exists. `test_the_verdict_compares_like_with_like_and_the_excursion_never_decides` moves the excursion across three bands and requires the verdict not to move. **An artifact that contradicts the better-powered reading of its own instrument is worse than one that says less**, which is why this is recorded as a correction rather than a refinement.
    - **No pooled range is written into the record, deliberately.** A pooled range is a constant, and a constant here would travel onto hosts it was never measured on — which is exactly what `what_this_is_a_property_of[0]` stopped being a hardcoded sentence to end. `test_no_pooled_range_from_another_run_is_installed_as_a_constant` is what notices one being added. The pooled half of limb ③ is carried in prose, below, where a human states its provenance.
  - ✅ **THE AUTHORITATIVE ENVIRONMENT PRODUCED A COMMITTED RECORD ON 2026-08-10, WHICH IT NEVER HAD.** `tests/batteries/results/seccomp-overhead-ci-31435892323.json` is run `31435892323`'s `pytest-outcomes-and-native-overhead` artifact, taken at `cc34adb`, downloaded and committed **byte-for-byte under a new name and not otherwise touched**. It is `kernel 6.17.0-1020-azure`, `machine x86_64`, **`euid 0`**, 4 vCPU, CPython 3.12.13 — the environment [OD-30](../001-discovery-validation/plan.md) selects, carrying the `euid` key blocker 4 records the committed artifact as lacking. **The route was the one the declined `workflow_dispatch` proposal was already found to have built**: a human causes a run, a human downloads that run's artifact and commits it. Nobody had walked it; it walks.
    - **The gitignore needed no restructuring, and the trap the removal-proof archive recorded does not arise here.** Line 160 is `tests/batteries/results/*.latest.json` — a *file* pattern inside a directory that is **not** excluded, so git descends and any name not ending `.latest.json` is trackable. `git check-ignore -v` on the new path returns nothing. **The run id is in the filename on purpose**: it is what makes the artifact's provenance re-derivable by a reader who has only the file, since `gh run download 31435892323` returns the same bytes, and it is what stops a later local privileged run from silently overwriting a CI figure with a linuxkit one.
    - **The 2026-08-03 record is KEPT, not replaced, and not edited.** The standing rule that the repair for a stale measurement is a new measurement decides the editing question by itself; the keep-or-delete question is decided separately, and on the ground that **the old file is the evidence for a claim this corpus makes about itself**. `seccomp-overhead.json` is three arms on `6.12.76-linuxkit`/`aarch64` with no `euid` field, and the reason blocker 4 and OD-30's environment comparison can be *read* rather than taken on trust is that the file they describe is still there to open. Deleting it would leave a corpus asserting that a stale unlabelled record existed and no way to check. Two records on different kernels **are not a before and an after and must not be subtracted**, and keeping both in one directory is exactly the situation that rule was written for.
  - ⚖️ **THE FIGURE, ALL FIVE ARMS, EACH WITH ITS RANGES ON ITS OWN LINE — run `31435892323`, `6.17.0-1020-azure` x86_64 at `euid 0`, `REPEATS = 5`.** This run's control excursion is **−0.000480 to +0.033130 s**; the pooled control range across the six recorded probe samples is **−0.013731 to +0.029317 s** (below).
    - `path_heavy` **+0.133442 s**, 64.22 µs/notification over 2078 notifications — clears this run's control (+0.016449 s) by **8.11×**, stands clear of this run's excursion, and stands clear of the pooled range.

    - `reference_app_socket` **+0.053384 s**, 64.09 µs over 833 — clears by **3.25×**, stands clear of this run's excursion, and stands clear of the pooled range.
    - `shell_heavy` **+0.027027 s**, 72.65 µs over 372 — clears by **1.64×**, the narrowest margin on this record, and **OVERLAPS this run's control excursion (−0.000480 to +0.033130 s) and OVERLAPS the pooled control range (−0.013731 to +0.029317 s)**: control draws on this very run roamed far enough to have produced a difference this size.

  - **The pooled control range, with its provenance, because the record deliberately does not carry it.** Six `seccomp-variance.latest.json` samples at k=10, 10 control draws each, 60 draws: `31415736559`, `31416789165`, `31416959913` (pooled −0.007711 to +0.029317, non-positive 9 of 30, recorded at `0236005`) together with `31427947131` (−0.012760 to +0.021070, non-positive 5), `31432640053` (−0.011675 to +0.009503, non-positive 6) and `31435892323` (−0.013731 to +0.018436, non-positive 3). **Pooled: −0.013731 to +0.029317 s, non-positive in 23 of 60 draws.** The pooled figure is a union of the recorded extremes and no run is subtracted from another. `shell_heavy` pooled over the same six is **+0.012572 to +0.028696 s** — a band lying wholly inside the pooled control range, which is the whole hazard in one line.
  - ⚠️ **THE SAMPLE TALLY MOVED AND THE HAZARD DID NOT: `shell_heavy` NOW CLEARS ITS OWN RUN'S CONTROL ON FOUR OF SIX RECORDED SAMPLES, AND IS STILL THE ONLY ONE OF THE FOUR LOAD-BEARING ARMS THAT OVERLAPS THE CONTROL AT ALL.** On the strict per-draw reading the bullet above uses — every one of the arm's 10 draws above the same run's control positive extreme — the three new samples read **10 of 10**, **10 of 10** and **10 of 10**, against the earlier three's 10 of 10, 1 of 10 and 0 of 10. `path_heavy`, `reference_app_api` and `reference_app_socket` clear on every draw of all six at minima of 0.058583, 0.023882 and 0.023358 s. **Three consecutive clears do not retire the finding and are not treated as having done so**: the arm's pooled band still sits inside the pooled control band, its two failures are recorded samples rather than discarded ones, and the run that produced the authoritative record is also the run on which it cleared by the narrowest margin any artifact carries. **The revision removed the withholding, not the hazard.**
  - ⚖️ **`shell_heavy`'s RATE IS PUBLISHED AND IS PUBLISHED QUALIFIED, NOT WITHHELD — decided 2026-08-10, and the `06cd176` precedent was tested against it rather than assumed to reach.** The question was whether **72.65 µs/notification** should stand bare given that the arm's pooled band lies wholly inside the pooled control band (provenance in the bullet above), that it is the only one of the four load-bearing arms overlapping the control at all, and that the authoritative run's margin is the narrowest that record carries.
    - **Withholding is refused, and the ground is that taking it would require installing the one constant this battery has spent four samples refusing.** `06cd176` withheld a rate into `UNRATED` because the **sign** of the difference made it not an overhead — a ruling from the quantity's *definition*, which needs no measurement and no threshold. Here the sign is fine and the objection is **magnitude**, and there is no magnitude this battery can name: `UNRATED["non-positive-overhead"]` says in terms that the boundary is zero *"because the quantity's definition forbids crossing it, and for no other reason"*, and `test_a_small_positive_overhead_is_still_published_because_no_floor_is_known` asserts the refusal so that anyone installing a bound has to delete the reasoning first. **The precedent does not reach.** It is the same instrument and the same field, and that is exactly what makes the difference load-bearing rather than cosmetic — a rule that withheld on magnitude would be a noise floor wearing the sign test's clothes, and the floor measurement that would justify one moved **2.9×** across three samples of identical construction on one runner class.
    - **Publishing bare is refused too, and this is the half the pass changed.** The figure is a measurement and not an absence, so it publishes; but `overhead_seconds` no longer travels without its qualification, and the rate is that same overhead divided by a count this battery has shown takes exactly one value within a run. A consumer holding the record can now reach `overhead_against_this_runs_control.qualified_by` and read `overlaps-this-runs-control-excursion` **without parsing prose**, and cannot lift the verdict as a bare string at all. That is what discharges the figure's exposure inside the record.
    - ⚠️ **The residual is named rather than claimed closed: `microseconds_per_notification` is itself still a bare number.** It is the field this module's own docstring calls *the transferable figure*, which makes it the most liftable thing in the record, and it is derived from a quantity that now carries a qualification it does not inherit mechanically. Nothing in this pass changed that, and the reason is scope rather than judgement — the rate is read as a number by two battery assertions, by `test_the_rate_is_re_derivable_from_the_two_fields_beside_it`, and by three declared removal proofs, so nesting it is a second schema change with its own proof obligations and belongs to a pass that can carry them. **Owner: whoever next takes T101.** Recorded here so the next reader inherits a stated gap rather than an unexamined field.
  - ⚠️ **T101 IS TICKED PARTIAL, AND WHAT IT OWES IS STATED HERE ONCE.** The exercised half is the criterion as written: the figure is recorded on all five arms from the authoritative environment at a stated kernel, architecture and euid, in a tracked artifact, with the same run's control beside every arm, any overlap stated on the line it qualifies, and a machine-readable verdict beside every figure so a consumer lifting `overhead_seconds` alone cannot lift it bare. **The unexercised half is that the magnitudes are a property of one run and Q-09's question is not.** Across-run spread on this same instrument is wide with no outlier, so no single run's median decides *prohibitive or not*; `shell_heavy`'s absolute overhead remains unresolved against the control; and the prohibitive-or-not ruling is an owner's, taken against the pooled evidence rather than against this record. **No threshold is installed and none is implied by the tick.** The tick is PARTIAL on the [T016](#storage-addressing-and-rollback) and T058 precedent: a clean tick here would read as though the decision the figure exists to inform had been taken.
- [X] T102 [US1] Session cgroup created and owned by the supervisor **before the container starts**, in `src/supervisor/cgroup.py` (FR-049)
  - **Extended 2026-08-03 by FR-049's pre-exec barrier clause, and this task as written does not discharge it.** Creating the cgroup before the container starts is one end; the other is that every bound is written before the workload process exists and the workload does not execute its first instruction until it is a member. The test that demonstrates it must show the workload **blocked before `execve`** and released only after membership — a test that spawns, attaches, and then observes the bound holding never enters the window and proves nothing about it
- [X] T103 [US1] The four controls — `memory.max` with `memory.oom.group`, `cpu.max` as a rate, cumulative `cpu.stat` against a declared total, and `pids.max` — in `src/supervisor/bounds.py` (FR-049; processor time is two bounds because **SC-023** asks two different things of one requirement, and `pids.max` is an addition marked as one because a fork bomb is the cheapest way to defeat the co-located-workload clause)
- [X] T104 [US1] No writable `cgroup` mount and no delegation inside the container, so nothing running inside can raise, extend or evade a bound, in `src/supervisor/cgroup.py` (FR-049's enforced-from-outside clause)
- [X] T105 [US1] Bound exhaustion ends the session in the matching named terminal state — `terminated.memory_bound_exhausted`, `terminated.cpu_bound_exhausted`, `terminated.process_bound_exhausted` — never by generic error, in `src/supervisor/bounds.py` (FR-049, FR-006)
- [X] T106 [P] [US1] Bounds battery: exhaust each declared bound in turn, asserting a named terminal state, that work already performed still counts against FR-005's ceilings, and that a co-located reference workload on the same host keeps serving throughout, in `tests/batteries/test_bounds_exhaustion.py` (**SC-023**)
- [X] T107 [US1] Capability handle as **opaque random bytes** — not a claim, not signed, nothing offline-verifiable — in `src/supervisor/capability.py` (FR-050 layer 1)
- [X] T108 [US1] Supervisor lease renewal on a short interval, with the proxy honouring `RUNNING` only while `lease_expires_at` is in the future, so that on a crash **nothing renews and the authority lapses without any code having run**, in `src/supervisor/lease.py` (FR-050 layer 2)
  - **The renewer stops permanently on the first exception of any kind, and the lease it was extending is already expired by the time anyone could look. Measured 2026-08-06; deliberately not repaired, because every available repair wants a decision this pass may not take.** `LeaseRenewer._loop` catches `Exception`, records it in `stopped_because` and **returns**. Planted arm — one `sqlite3.OperationalError("database is locked")` raised on the second of twelve scheduled renewals, real table, real thread, everything else untouched: renewals stop at **1 of 12**, the thread is dead, the row is still `RUNNING`, and the lease is **0.5 s in the past**. Control arm, the same probe with nothing planted: **12 of 12**, thread alive, lease in the future. So a momentary `SQLITE_BUSY` — precisely the class the repository layer labels *retrying is reasonable* — revokes a **healthy** session's authority. Lapsing is the fail-closed direction so nothing is unsafe, but it is wrong, and it is undiagnosable: `session_table.py`'s constructor comment already names this exact shape as *"its own defect"* after it cost real time once, when the default same-thread guard's `ProgrammingError` killed this thread silently. One cause was fixed there (`check_same_thread=False`); the general shape was left.
  - **The open question, put rather than answered.** The one-line counterfactual — `continue` in place of `return` — was measured on the same probe: **11 of 12** renewals, thread alive, lease healthy. It works because `LEASE_TTL_MULTIPLE = 2.0` **already** budgets for exactly one missed renewal, and the current loop cannot use the tolerance its own constant was chosen to provide. But `continue` retries **forever**, which is the trap the three-way split at `ff202ae` exists to prevent: `StoreBusyError`, `StoreWedgedError` and `StoreUnusableError` were introduced so a caller need not choose between giving up on the first contention and spinning on a wedged resource. **`SessionTable` raises none of them** — it raises raw `sqlite3.OperationalError`, because it sits outside the repository layer ([T016](#storage-addressing-and-rollback)). So each live option costs something: *catch the engine exception here*, which puts engine coupling into a file that is **not** on obligation 2's skip list; *bound the retries*, which is a new unmeasured number in a module whose docstring deliberately declined to add a second one (FR-043, T033); *make the thread die loudly*, ~~which has no vehicle — `src/` contains no `logging`, no `getLogger` and no `warnings.warn` anywhere~~ **whose premise is true and whose conclusion is not — corrected 2026-08-06 in the sub-bullet below**; or *complete T016's migration* and consume the classification that already exists. **The fourth is the one the other three are working around.** This is independent of the WAL race and of the migration in the sense that matters — a renewer that dies silently on any transient exception is wrong whichever layer the table sits behind — but not independent in the sense that would let it be fixed cheaply here. Reachability is the same as everything else in this module: `LeaseRenewer` is constructed only in `tests/integration/test_lease_revocation.py`, never in `src/`. ~~**No test is added**, on finding 033's ground: a test pinning the current behaviour would enshrine the defect and turn the repair into a failure, and a test pinning the desired behaviour presupposes the decision above.~~ **Superseded 2026-08-06: the decision was taken and a test now pins the *desired* behaviour — see the repair bullets below.** Reproduction is a dozen lines — wrap a real `SessionTable`'s `renew` so the *n*-th call raises `sqlite3.OperationalError`, start a real `LeaseRenewer`, sleep past several intervals, and read `renewals`, the thread's liveness and `lease_expires_at - time.time()`.
  - **Correction, measured 2026-08-06: "die loudly" *does* have a vehicle, and the option was ruled out on a search of files rather than on behaviour.** The premise above is exact and reproduces — an AST walk over all 60 files in Python `src/` and an independent regex sweep each return **zero** matches for `logging`, `getLogger` and `warnings.warn`. The conclusion drawn from it does not follow, and it is the failure `tools/README.md` names: a claim about **behaviour** whose evidence is a **file**. Planted and observed with fd-level capture: an uncaught exception on a **daemon** thread reaches stderr through `threading.excepthook` — **490 bytes** of traceback carrying the planted message, thread dead, process alive. So `_loop`'s `except Exception` is not compensating for a missing facility; **it is what closes a channel the interpreter already provides.** Deleting it makes the renewer die loudly with nothing added. Measured on the same probe for contrast: with the `except` in place, the identical planted failure emits **0 bytes** to stdout and stderr, and the reason survives only on `stopped_because` — an attribute **nothing in `src/` reads**, whose one reader anywhere is `tests/integration/test_lease_revocation.py`. That is strictly worse than the other two operator reports in the tree (`config.py:_report()`, `preflight.py:preflight()`), which at least hand their text to an exception.

So the three live options are an eighth span kind, which amends a closed set; an injected logger on Go's pattern, which the entry point recorded at [the configuration section](#configuration-and-failing-closed) does not yet exist to inject from; or the re-raise, accepting a traceback as the interface. ~~**The question is put, not answered**~~ **Answered 2026-08-06 — the third, as an interim; see the next bullet**, and no logging facility is built here: its shape — stdlib versus structured, and its relationship to the trace record and to the decision log Go already writes — is an owner's decision, not a recording pass's.
  - **⚠️ DECIDED and REPAIRED 2026-08-06 — the *re-raise* is taken, explicitly as an interim. The other three routes remain blocked and the fifth is ruled elsewhere.** The owner ruled it on three grounds: the lease lapses either way, so the change is **behaviour-neutral on the outcome** and converts a silent lapse into a visible one rather than altering what happens; `session_table.py`'s constructor comment already rules an undiagnosable lapse *"its own defect"*, which makes the visibility the missing half and not an addition; and it is reversible the moment an entry point exists to inject from. **Reproduced before acting rather than taken from the bullets above**, on this file's own rule that a fix verified against an unobserved failure is not verified — planted arm **1 of 12** renewals, thread dead, row `RUNNING`, lease **0.503 s in the past**, **0 bytes** on stdout and stderr; control arm **12 of 12**, thread alive, lease **+0.083 s**. After the change the same planted arm gives the **identical** 1 of 12, dead thread, `RUNNING` row and 0.505 s-expired lease, plus **881 bytes** of traceback naming the planted engine error — which is the whole of what the change buys, and why "interim" is accurate rather than modest. **The other three stay blocked for the reasons already recorded**: `continue` retries forever and `SessionTable` cannot raise `ff202ae`'s three error types from outside the repository layer; catching the engine exception here puts engine coupling into a file **not** on obligation 2's skip list; bounding the retries mints an unmeasured number in a module that deliberately declined to add a second one. **The fifth — completing T016's migration — is ruled deferred by [OD-28](../001-discovery-validation/plan.md#od-28--the-sessiontable--repository-migration-stays-deferred-and-the-shape-is-routed-to-t016s-note-rather-than-re-litigated-the-deferral-expires-the-moment-a-supervisor-process-constructs-a-session-store) and routed to T016's note; it is not re-litigated here.** **Nothing is re-estimated and no task is minted**, on [T041](#phase-3-foundational-b--the-runtime-core-od-15-left-unowned-u-48)'s ground that a re-estimate taken mid-phase by the pass that just changed the thing produces a motivated number.
  - **The channel's own limit, measured — because "the excepthook delivers it" is not true unconditionally, and the correction above did not test the case where it fails.** `7e874d7` established the vehicle on a daemon thread **mid-run**, which reproduces. It does **not** hold across interpreter finalization: `threading.excepthook` writes to a *buffered* stderr, so a raise coinciding with the main thread's exit is truncated, lost outright, or **aborts the process** — `Fatal Python error: _enter_buffered_busy`, SIGABRT, exit 134. Sweeping the main thread's exit across the raise instant in 0.5 ms steps (n=87): **41 clean, 14 truncated, 32 silent, 4 aborted**. Forcing the overlap with an event instead of sweeping for it: 20 of 20 truncated-or-aborted at 0 ms, **19 of 20 silent at 1 ms**, 20 of 20 silent at 5 ms and beyond. **Reachability is this module's usual and is stated so the figure is not overread**: nothing in `src/` constructs a `LeaseRenewer`, and the one real-process construction in the tree — the `SIGKILL` fixture's child — never exits normally, so the window is not reachable today. It is recorded because it is a **further argument that a traceback is not the operator interface**, not an argument for the swallow, which loses the report in *every* case rather than in a sub-millisecond one. **The durable answer is unchanged: a logger injected from an entry point on Go's pattern** — `log.New(os.Stderr, ...)` as the first statement of `main()` in `src/proxy/main.go`, handed downward, with no package-level logger anywhere in Go — blocked on the seam at [the configuration section](#configuration-and-failing-closed). No logging facility is built here, and the comment at the change site says so in terms.
  - **`stopped_because` is kept and set *before* the raise, and the reasoning is not "setting-then-raising is obviously right".** Its exception-path value has **no reader at all**, not even a test — checked across the tree including dynamic access. But the *attribute* has two: `test_the_renewer_thread_actually_renews` interpolates it into its failure message and asserts it `None` on the healthy path, and `test_the_renewer_says_why_it_stopped` asserts the `"session is no longer RUNNING"` value — which is the **orderly** stop and not this defect. So removing the attribute would delete a correct test to repair an unrelated one, and dropping only the exception-path assignment would make the attribute lie: its documented `None` means *running, or stopped in the orderly way*, and a crash leaving it `None` reports an orderly stop to the one reader it has. `7e874d7`'s charge that it is *"strictly worse than the other two operator reports, which at least hand their text to an exception"* is **discharged by setting *and* raising**, not by deletion. **Blast radius, established rather than assumed**: no existing test asserted the swallow — nothing in the suite plants an exception on this path, which is precisely why the fix was removable in silence; the exception cannot take down anything but the renewer thread outside the finalization window above; and under `pytest` the same exception surfaces as `PytestUnhandledThreadExceptionWarning` rather than an error, there being no `filterwarnings = error` in this repository's configuration.
  - **The proof, added because the fix was removable with the suite still green.** Restoring `return` in place of `raise` left every test passing, so `test_a_failed_renewal_is_not_silent` is added to `tests/integration/test_lease_revocation.py` and declared in `tests/removal_proofs.sh`; `EXPECTED_PROOFS` moves **163 → 164**, read off the guard's own transition message rather than computed from a baseline and a delta. The arm runs the renewer **out of process** on purpose — `pytest` installs its own `threading.excepthook` and demotes thread exceptions to warnings, so an in-process assertion would score the plugin and not the channel a deployed supervisor has — and its child outlives the raise by an order of magnitude, keeping it clear of the finalization window. **It discriminates**: under the tamper its `RENEWALS 1` assertion still passes and only the stderr assertion moves, so it proves the *report* rather than the renewal count, which is the same arm either way.
  - **⚠️ THE DURABLE REPAIR, TAKEN 2026-08-06 — the interim's blocking reason expired with T016's migration, and the re-raise is *kept* as the terminal branch rather than retired.** Two of the four blocked routes were blocked *by* that migration being undone, and both fell with it: *`continue` retries forever* and *`SessionTable` cannot raise `ff202ae`'s three error types from outside the repository layer*. `_loop` now tolerates `StoreBusyError` and stops on everything else. **The bound is derived, not minted** — which is what separates it from *bound the retries*, the option this entry refused for adding an unmeasured number: `TOLERATED_CONSECUTIVE_BUSY = int(LEASE_TTL_MULTIPLE) - 1`, because the lease is granted for that many intervals and tolerating one more would let a renewal succeed **after its own lease had lapsed**, reviving authority FR-050 says is already gone. It is a safety property of the lease, and it carries no number of its own.
  - **Why `continue` is bounded here, measured rather than argued — this is the limb the earlier refusal turned on.** *Retries forever* would be right if a permanently held lock produced an endless stream of `StoreBusyError`. It does not, and the reason is a property of the store rather than of this loop: `StoreBusyError` means SQLite refused **without waiting**, which it does only where waiting could deadlock, whereas an ordinary statement gets the busy handler — so a lock that is never released **exhausts** it and reads as `StoreWedgedError`. Planted at both lock levels with the timeout in force, five consecutive renewals against a permanently held lock returned `StoreWedgedError` **5 of 5 at each level**, every one having waited out the whole timeout. **A permanently held lock cannot reach the tolerant branch from this call site.** The derived bound holds anyway and is asserted against a planted source that cannot occur.
  - **Counterfactual on the real class, one planted momentary failure on renewal 2 of 12.** Re-raise-everything (the interim): **1 of 12**, thread dead, lease **0.501 s in the past**. This loop: **10 of 12**, thread alive, lease **+0.046 s**, `stopped_because` `None`. Control with nothing planted: **12 of 12**. A planted `StoreWedgedError` still gives 1 of 12 and a dead thread, which is the split working rather than the repair failing.
  - **The re-raise is kept and is no longer an interim; its removal proof and its interim comment stay with it.** It is now the *terminal* branch — reached where retrying cannot help — and the arm that proves it (`test_a_failed_renewal_is_not_silent`) plants a raw `sqlite3.OperationalError`, which after the migration is not a store error at all and so can only reach that branch. Its proof's tamper is unchanged and still targets it uniquely. **What is retired is the word *interim*, not the mechanism**: ~~the durable operator interface is still a logger injected from an entry point on Go's pattern, still blocked on the same seam, and the finalization window recorded above is unchanged~~ and is still not an argument for the swallow.
  - **⚠️ The vehicle arrived 2026-08-08 with T211, and it arrived somewhere this bullet did not predict — `lease.py` is not modified and does not import it.** Every route considered above assumed the renewer would be *handed* a logger. The one built is installed at the **delivery end**: `OperatorLog.adopt_thread_exceptions()` replaces `threading.excepthook` with a hook that writes through a single unbuffered `os.write`, and the entry points call it before any thread starts, so one installation covers every thread the process will ever have — including the ones no constructor here is passed. **That is why the renewer's `raise` needed no change to acquire an operator interface.** The finalization window narrowed by measurement rather than by argument, on a design that forces the overlap instead of sweeping for it: the stock hook aborts the process in **7 of 120** trials and the adopted one in **0 of 120**; a direct buffered write from a daemon thread aborts in **38 of 40** and the unbuffered write in **0 of 40**. **The abort and the truncation are gone; the silent case is not, and it belongs to neither hook** — swept in 0.5 ms steps both are silent in 20 of 20 at 0 ms and clean from 0.5 ms onward, because a thread that was never scheduled has nothing to deliver. So the 87-trial sweep above is superseded on three of its four outcomes and stands on the fourth. **Nothing is re-estimated**, on [T041](#phase-3-foundational-b--the-runtime-core-od-15-left-unowned-u-48)'s ground.
  - **Four proofs, because the branch is four mechanisms that fail in four directions.** Collapse to re-raise-everything, tolerate without bound, widen the split from `StoreBusyError` to its base `StoreUnavailableError`, and drop the consecutive-counter reset — each fails a different arm, and the arms assert **attempt counts** rather than words in a message. `EXPECTED_PROOFS` moves **182 → 192** with the six T016 per-row-scope proofs, read off the guard's own transition message and updated in the same commit.
