### The runtime's own plane, the batteries, and the SC-001 path

- [X] T113 [US1] The runtime's own default-deny egress plane pinned to the model provider, in `src/runtime/egress.py` (T-10 — an addition beyond what the specification requires, because FR-014 through FR-019 scope to the execution environment and the principle's concern is the process that puts attacker-influenceable text into a model)
  - **The row was not stale and is not struck** (the T082 shape). What it did not carry, and now does, is **the authority the addition rests on**: [`.specify/memory/constitution.md`](../../.specify/memory/constitution.md) **Principle IV, bullet 1**, whose four terms are pinned addresses, host-and-port granularity, DNS denied, and the named address classes denied *even on an allowlisted host* — and which states that a configuration missing **any one** of the four does not satisfy it. So there is one removal proof per term, which is what the authority asks for rather than thoroughness for its own sake. **No FR is cited and none was invented.** In particular **FR-050 is not cited**: it is a credential-lifetime requirement whose four layers are opaque handle, lease row, held descriptor and resume-renews-not-reissues, only the third of which is kernel, and it has been mis-cited for egress before. `test_no_runtime_egress_rule_cites_a_functional_requirement` fails if any `RTE-` rule acquires an `FR-` string, so the addition cannot quietly grow a requirement citation later
  - **Identifiers are `RTE-`, a new namespace.** `EG-` is the egress policy's and `src/proxy/policy.go` refuses an artifact carrying a registered one; `ADM-`, `DEP-`, `FS-`, `EFF-OP-` and `EFF-DENY-` are taken. `test_the_runtime_egress_namespace_collides_with_no_other_registry` is the guard
  - ⚠️ **This does NOT narrow C-16's exfiltration limb, and the reason is worth stating rather than glossing.** The runtime has **no outbound transport at all** — `src/runtime/providers/wire_*.py` are wire-format adapters, and no `httpx`, `requests`, `urllib`, `http.client` or `aiohttp` call site exists anywhere under `src/runtime/`. The plane is also **not installed by `src/runtime/main.py`**, which resolves configuration and returns a readiness report rather than serving, so there is no long-lived process to install it in; installing it there would also patch `socket` process-wide for the whole test suite. A mechanism nobody reaches narrows nothing, and claiming otherwise is the overclaim this row refuses to make
  - ⚠️ **And do not read the bullet above as "wire it up and C-16 narrows" — that inference is false.** C-16's exfiltration limb has an **enumerated** residue after OD-12 closed the direct channel: the target application as a confused deputy for outbound requests (**U-44**), the response channel back to the operator's own client, and delayed egress through artifacts the agent writes into shared storage. T113 is on **none** of the three. It is a control on a fourth channel C-16 never enumerated, the runtime process's own sockets, which exists because Principle IV's scope is the process that puts attacker-influenceable text into a model while FR-014–FR-019 scope to the execution environment. Installing the plane would close a gap in the **principle's** coverage; it would leave all three of C-16's enumerated residues exactly where they are
  - **What is live today is the interception point, and it is static.** `test_no_runtime_module_originates_a_connection_around_the_plane` scans every module under `src/runtime/` except `egress.py` and fails on any outbound call site, so the T058 provider transport **cannot be written unguarded** — the way to make it pass is to route it through `guarded()` with a pinned `Destination`. Two floors, because the assertion succeeds by finding nothing: a planted-call control proves the matcher can match, and a population floor (34 modules scanned, floor 20) proves it was pointed at something. A third arm proves the one exclusion, `sqlite3.connect`, is a symbol and not a rule about the word `connect`
  - **The API is fail-closed and the classifier is positive.** `require_installed()` raises when no plane is installed rather than returning `None`; only `AF_INET` and `AF_INET6` can be permitted and a family outside that named set is **denied, not passed through**; a `Destination` must hold a parsed address and an explicit port, so a name cannot be pinned and a port cannot be defaulted. Denied classes are checked **twice** — at declaration, so loopback and the metadata address cannot be pinned at all, and at connect, which is the "even on an allowlisted host" half — and the two proofs for term 4 are isolated so that each tamper leaves the other check intact
- [X] T114 [P] [US1] Adversarial battery for **SC-002** and **SC-003**: zero calls that did not resolve read-only reach the target, 100% of denials state their rule, zero outbound connections leave for a destination outside the pinned set including those a command opened itself, and zero requests allowed whose method could not be read, in `tests/batteries/test_adversarial_egress.py`
  - **The target now exists (T116).** It publishes a `served_operation_set` and holds **exactly one** operation that is not read-only, `POST /shipments/{shipment_id}/cancel`. That count is itself under a floor — `test_exactly_one_published_operation_is_not_read_only` — because a battery asserting that nothing non-read-only reaches a target with nothing non-read-only in it passes while covering nothing. The route table and the published specification are reconciled in both directions, so an operation the specification does not describe is refused by rule (`REFAPP-001`) rather than guessed at
  - ✅ **Landed 2026-08-08.** The battery stands the *assembled* enforcement point up — the Go binary, a per-run TLS origin serving T116 at a pinned address, a policy **derived** from `served_operations.json` rather than written by hand, and a live session row — and drives 18 arms through it. The oracle is the **target's own record of what it served**, not the proxy's record of what it forwarded. Both of Rule 8's second readings were observed and are written to `tests/batteries/results/`: the SC-002 control sends the cancel operation *straight at the origin*, and the same detector names `POST /shipments/S-0001/cancel → write`; the SC-003 control runs the same prober *outside* the network namespace and the same detector names the address it reached
  - ⚠️ **Two things this does not measure, stated so a green run is not over-read.** **First, SC-003's other half.** The topological arm models the sandbox's side only — a workload in a namespace with no interfaces reaches nothing it composes itself. It does **not** show that the enforcement point stays reachable over exactly one route, because that needs a `veth` pair and a container network `deploy/compose/` does not yet build. **Second, the widened-policy mechanism proof does not exist**, and the reason is a product property rather than a gap: `ParsePolicy` refuses a `method_allowlist` naming a non-safe method (FR-009), so the tamper that would let a write through produces a proxy that never listens rather than a proxy that forwards
- [X] T115 [P] [US1] Adversarial battery for **SC-022**: zero reads and zero writes succeed outside the declared set — including against the effect-gate rule set, the egress policy and another session's artifacts — zero partially succeed, and 100% of refusals are recorded with their rule, in `tests/batteries/test_adversarial_filesystem.py`
  - **Scored on the record's existence and its rule, per SC-022's 2026-08-03 narrowing.** No arm may assert that the recorded `path` equals the path the adversary asked for: the supervisor reads that path out of the workload's own memory, so a workload rewriting it between the read and the kernel's resolution misattributes an audit entry — and obtains no access, because the mount namespace makes an undeclared path absent. An arm asserting path equality would fail against a correctly-behaving supervisor. The `path` is best-effort and carries its provenance; the rule identifier is the supervisor's own and is what this battery scores
  - **The subject now exists (T116).** The reference application reads and writes nothing outside `state_root()`, which has no environment variable that moves it — a location that can be reconfigured is a location a battery cannot make claims about — so the declared set has a single member and everything else is an adversarial arm. `Application` holds a private copy of its state, so a write arm cannot silently mutate the control arm it is diffed against
  - ✅ **Landed 2026-08-08, and it is the first *measurement* behind [OD-24](../001-discovery-validation/plan.md) ground ①.** Fourteen arms run inside a real per-session mount namespace with the reference application live inside it — the workload reproduces all four evidence digests, so the containment result is not a statement about an empty box. The positive control declares an extra `leak` location for the namespace build and **not** for the set under test, so its reads and writes genuinely succeed outside that set and the same `violations()` names both by path. Ground ① claims the landed mount repairs close [finding 021](./findings/021-openat2-audit-gap-and-two-authority-gaps.md)'s two authority gaps; both repairs now carry a removal proof scored by a *workload* rather than by a hand-written write, and removing either lets the write land. **Nothing escaped**, which supports ground ① on the arms it covers and leaves the privilege-model build deferred where OD-24 put it
  - ⚠️ **Two instrument defects this battery found in itself, both worth carrying.** **First, the declared location is bound from a per-run copy, never from the committed fixture.** The target must be the real `tests/fixtures/reference-app/` path or the application cannot find its state — but a removal proof deliberately removes the read-only remount, and the write then lands. Bound from the real directory it landed on the repository's own `state.json`, corrupting every later proof that reads the reference application: four proofs read `BROKEN` in the run of 2026-08-08 for that and for nothing else. **Second, a positive control must not `print`.** Capture is off here, and `removal_proofs.sh` reads a baseline verdict off the same line as the node id, so a test that writes to stdout puts `PASSED` on a later line and **every proof aimed at it is scored `SKIPPED` and never attempted**. The evidence goes to `tests/batteries/results/` instead; `tests/batteries/evidence.py` carries the reasoning
- [X] T116 [US1] Reference application with seeded state and known-correct answers, plus its **stated size**, in `tests/fixtures/reference-app/` (FR-053)
  - **The stated size, measured by `size.py` and asserted against `README.md`**: 3 application files, 606 lines, 442 non-blank non-comment lines, 32 `def`/`class` definitions, over 11 seeded parts and 44 seeded shipments, 5 served operations and 4 questions. `codegraph_nodes` and `codegraph_edges` are deliberately `null` — `codegraph` is invoked by T119, which does not exist, so **no node count for this application has ever been taken** and nothing here converts lines into nodes using **U-21**'s single datapoint. T203 must report the figures above and that gap, not a derived node count. **This application is small and is not a scale datapoint**; it does not test U-21's open question
  - **"Known-correct" is two things, and only one of them is forgeable.** Each question carries an `answer` — a fold over the served business fields, reproducible by anything that can read prices and quantities — *and* an `evidence_digest` over the `attestation` values of exactly the records the answer depends on. An attestation is `HMAC-SHA256(ATTESTATION_KEY, {kind, id, epoch})` and covers **no business field**, so no served field determines it and no operation discloses the key. The proof that this works is a negative control: `test_the_lossy_oracle_gets_every_answer_right_and_every_digest_wrong` reaches the correct records, drops their attestations and recomputes each answer — **4/4 on answers, 0/4 on digests**. That gap is finding 016's blindness, measured rather than asserted. The scope is unforgeability *from the served surface*, not from the repository: the key is committed so the fixture is reproducible
  - **Synthetic throughout.** Every record is produced from its own index by arithmetic; nothing was chosen by looking at the questions. `PART_COUNT = 11` and the three-element status cycle are coprime **on purpose** — the first draft used 12, which shares a factor with 3, and two of the four questions silently degenerated to an empty evidence set with the answer zero while every answer *and* digest assertion still passed. `test_no_question_has_an_empty_evidence_set` is the floor, and it runs over the generated questions rather than the committed ones because the arithmetic is where the defect was
- [X] T117 [US1] Unattended first-verified-answer harness measuring the **SC-001** path from starting configuration, in `tests/integration/test_sc001_first_answer.py`
  - ⚠️ **The task text is intact and the requirement WIDENED around it — nothing here is struck, and the gap is recorded rather than corrected.** T117 as written asks for a harness that measures the SC-001 path. What the text does not carry, and what the specification acquired with FR-045's 2026-08-03 window-and-surface statement, is the passage at spec.md ~2559: *"SC-001 asks for a first verified answer within fifteen minutes, which a high refusal share can defeat without anything in the runtime being wrong — so SC-001 is not independently assessable until FR-045 has reported at least once."* A harness built to the task text alone would report a green SC-001 and be wrong about what it had shown. **Built so that extending is a constant** ([T082](#phase-4-user-story-1--operate-a-running-application-through-its-own-specification-safely-priority-p1--mvp)'s precedent): `assessable` is *derived* from FR-045's window being a production one, so the day FR-045 reports for real the flag flips with no edit here.
  - **Two routes into a vacuously green SC-001, both closed, and they are closed differently because they fail differently.** *Quoting a timing with no share beside it* is closed **structurally** — `Sc001Report` will not construct without FR-045's share or without the subject's size, so there is no artifact this harness can emit that carries a verdict alone. *Stopping at the first verified answer* is closed in the **run**: `_run` attempts every question, because a run that stopped would divide the share by however many questions it reached before getting lucky, and Q-001 verifies first.
  - **The negative control, and it is a control rather than a broken origin.** A lossy origin drops the attestations for one record kind and touches no business field. Every question is still **answered correctly under it** — asserted directly, by recomputing all four answers from what the lossy origin served — and half of them become unverifiable. The result: the **same** `first_verified_answer_within_window` verdict as the clean run, a `0.5` not-verifiable share, and the share is the only thing carrying the difference. That is the vacuity made observable rather than argued about.
  - **Absent evidence is recorded `not_verifiable`, never `failed`, and the constant is named once** (`ABSENT_EVIDENCE_OUTCOME`) so the choice is one edit rather than two branches that drift. FR-045's share is a share of the *not-verifiable* state, so a refusal booked as a failure costs the run its answer and leaves the share anyway — which is the one way to get a clean share out of a run that verified nothing. Its removal proof is that exact swap.
  - ⚠️ **Three things a green run here does not show, stated so it is not over-read.** **No model provider is in this path** — the answering step recomputes from the served surface, and an agent loop would add unbounded time this does not measure. **No enforcement point is in this path**: an in-process HTTP origin on loopback, no network and no TLS; T114 is the battery that stands the assembled point up. And **this is not FR-022's independent recomputation** — both sides descend from `seed.py`'s arithmetic, so it catches a routing, filtering, serialization or opaque-state-dropping defect and not a wrong seed, which `seed.py`'s own docstring already disclaims about its expected answers.
  - **Measured 2026-08-10 and recorded** in `tests/batteries/results/sc001-first-answer.json`: first verified answer at **0.0162 s** of a **0.518 s** window against SC-001's 900 s, 4 of 4 verified, not-verifiable share **0.0**, `assessable: false`. Environment: **macOS 26.2 arm64, CPython 3.12.11, unprivileged (uid 501)**. The verdict is `first_verified_answer_within_window` and it is **not** a claim that SC-001 is met.
- [X] T118 [US1] **Instrument analysis wall time separately from the rest of the SC-001 window, and state the reference application's size wherever SC-001 is reported**, in `src/analysis/timing.py` — SC-001 is a compound of a bounded step and an unbounded one, **U-21** records `codegraph`'s scale claim as untested, and reporting the two together is what makes the criterion quietly true on small inputs and quietly false on large ones
  - **Nothing here was stale.** Alone among this group, T118's text was already current with every requirement that landed after it: it names U-21, the compound, and the size clause, and no later requirement falsified or widened any of them. Recorded because a task whose text needed no correction is worth distinguishing from one nobody checked.
  - **The split is a refusal, not a convention.** `Sc001Timer.close()` fails on a window in which no analysis span was recorded, naming it a *fused total* — because a window with a zero analysis span is arithmetically consistent and nothing else in the module would refuse it. `remainder_seconds` is a named field rather than left to subtraction, since the subtraction is exactly what nobody performs when they read one number.
  - **The size is required and is not defaulted anywhere.** `SubjectSize.from_document` refuses a `size.json` missing any field rather than filling the hole, because a hole is the two artifacts having drifted apart and filling it hides that at the moment it matters. `codegraph_nodes` stays `None` and a unit test fails if it ever grows a number — **no arithmetic here turns a line count into a node count** on U-21's single datapoint.
  - ⚠️ **Sound with [T004](#phase-1-setup-shared-infrastructure) outstanding as an *instrument*, and explicitly not sound as a *datapoint* — which is the whole reason the marking is load-bearing.** T004 leaves `CODEGRAPH_SCHEMA_SHA256` unset so the pin fails closed, and T119, which would invoke `codegraph` at all, does not exist. So an analysis span taken today times a step that **excludes the unbounded work U-21 is about**. Keeping the two spans apart and forcing the denominator does not depend on `codegraph` having run; treating the resulting `analysis_seconds` as evidence about scale does. `codegraph_invoked` therefore defaults `False`, the payload carries the caveat naming T004, T119 and U-21, and a removal proof fires if the default is flipped. The failure being prevented is concrete: a later reader finding a small `analysis_seconds` in a committed artifact and reading it as *analysis is cheap*.
  - **No threshold is applied to FR-045's share and none is invented**, which the payload states in its own `threshold_note` rather than only by omission. The denominator is the **attempted** set, and the breakdown must account for its own total; both are refusals with their own proofs, because a share over an empty population is not a low share and a breakdown that does not sum describes neither of its numbers.
  - **Ten removal proofs, one per refusal, all observed** — `294 proved, 0 unproven, 13 skipped` on macOS 26.2 arm64 unprivileged at uid 501. **None came back UNPROVEN from double coverage**, which is the outcome this group expected and did not get: the ten are ten independent guards, so removing any one leaves the other nine reporting and only its own arm moves.

**Checkpoint**: User Story 1 is independently testable — a verified answer on the reference
application, with every adversarial arm denied and recorded.

---

## Phase 5: User Story 2 — Know whether the answer was actually right (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: for every reported result, recompute the reported quantity by a path independent of the
one that produced it, and report exactly one of three states with the state visible to the caller.

**Independent Test**: a corpus of results with injected value faults including near-misses smaller
than one percent of the correct value, and a matched corpus of clean results. Report detection rate
and false-alarm rate. Separately, confirm a verifier restricted to shape and type conformance
detects **none** of the value faults — the control that demonstrates detection comes from
recomputation rather than from conformance checking.

- [X] T119 [US2] `codegraph` invoked as a subprocess at analysis time only, absent from every run-time image, in `src/analysis/codegraph.py` (T-11, **D-14**)
  - ⚠️ **PARTIAL 2026-08-12, on T016's, T058's and T101's precedent, and the unexercised half is named because a clean tick over it is the rot this file warns about.** *Exercised:* the argv and its subcommand; the runner called exactly once with the repository as cwd; a non-zero exit carrying the indexer's stderr; **a zero exit with no artifact**, which a returncode check alone reads as success; the pin asserted **before** the index is returned, with `CodegraphIndex.verified` the only constructor so there is no value of the type holding an unchecked artifact; no run-time module importing it, with the scan verified against a planted edge **and** against `codegraph_pin`, which is legitimately reachable and must not be caught by the prefix; the sandbox image carrying no JavaScript toolchain, with the scan verified against a planted `apt-get install nodejs npm` and verified to keep excusing the existing `rm -rf /root/.npmrc`; and every `subprocess` reference confined to one named function, verified by planting a second call site. *Outstanding:* **no test in this repository runs a real `codegraph`.** Every arm injects the subprocess boundary, which is deliberate — `examples/codegraph` is git-ignored and building it needs `npm install`, so a shelling-out test would skip on almost every machine, and a silently skipping test is the vacuity pattern this corpus has hardened roughly ten instruments against. What that leaves unexercised is the argv actually producing an artifact. The evidence for that is [`harness/recall-adk-fastapi/run.sh`](../001-discovery-validation/harness/recall-adk-fastapi/run.sh), the committed recipe that produced the artifact T004's digest was read from, and it is evidence of a procedure rather than a passing test
  - **Two removal proofs, and the second is the one a returncode check hides.** Swapping `pin.verify` for `pin.schema_digest` leaves the index constructible and the digest populated and removes only the comparison, turning exactly one named test red; disabling the `is_file` guard admits a successful run that wrote nothing. Both were observed failing by planting before they were written down
- [X] T120 [US2] Static derivation of contracts and checks from source with **no model call anywhere in it**, in `src/analysis/derive.py` (T-13, FR-023 — static derivation was measured at zero model spend, so this costs nothing that was ever measured to be worth having)
  - **Done 2026-08-12. The derivation emits checks capable of expressing a recomputation, which is the property T124 and T132 depend on and the reason the task is not satisfied by a shape extractor.** Five named rules over Python's own `ast` — `return_annotation`, `aggregate_binding`, `postcondition_assert`, `precondition_guard`, `raises_statement` — each registered in `provenance.DERIVATION_RULES` with the FR-023 artifact class it reads, so an unregistered rule cannot reach an artifact. `CheckKind` has two members: `SHAPE`, which is what feature 001's ~~**8 schema-blind numeric false successes**~~ **9 schema-blind numeric false successes** passed — *corrected 2026-08-12 from [`E8-VIABILITY.md`](../001-discovery-validation/E8-VIABILITY.md) §6: the split is **9 numeric and 2 set-typed**, not 8 and 3, and the schema-derived arm is blind to both classes rather than only to the numeric one, so the struck figure named a narrower blind spot than the measured one. The task disposition above is unchanged; only the quoted census figure moves* — and `RECOMPUTATION`, which carries a structured `Recomputation(operator, over, element_field, reads)` the verifier can execute without parsing prose. **Independence is a constructor rule**: a recomputation whose `reads` contains the quantity under check is refused, because it agrees with itself whatever the value is
  - **Why `ast` and not the `codegraph` index, since **D-14** points the other way.** Measured rather than preferred: [finding 007](../001-discovery-validation/findings/007-contract-extraction.md) records that Python reached 100% on parameters *only* because the harness walks models with `ast`, **capability that does not exist in the index**, and that the index's dedicated `return_type` column is empty across all 48,154 Python nodes. The index is the symbol graph; the contract is in the syntax. T119 and this are complementary and neither substitutes for the other — **and note that this means T120 does not currently consume T119's output at all**, which is a wiring gap owed to whoever builds the analysis stage proper
  - **No rule reads a docstring**, on **U-27**: finding 004 measured that field populated with *wrong* values rather than left empty, which is a worse risk class than a coverage gap
  - **"No model call anywhere in it" is checked over the transitive first-party import closure**, not one level — a derivation importing a helper that imports a provider adapter still has a model in it — and the scan is verified to fire at **one hop and at two**, the second being the arm a one-level scan would call clean
  - **A function no rule fires on produces no contract.** That default is load-bearing and has its own proof: an analyzer that emits something for everything it sees cannot be told apart from one that is right, and *fluent, plausible and wrong* is the failure class this corpus has measured twice by two different mechanisms
  - ⚠️ **Scope, stated so the tick is not over-read.** Five rules over hand-written synthetic Python. No framework, no decorators, no route table, no second language, two files. Finding 007's **0.8696** literal and **0.7681** validated figures are about a real framework's published schema and are **not** a claim about this module
- [X] T121 [US2] Provenance as data on every derived contract and check — the derivation rule, the source symbol, the source file, the analyzer version, a content hash and a validation status — in `src/analysis/provenance.py` (FR-026)
  - **Done 2026-08-12. All six fields, each refusing something, because "carry as data" is satisfied by a field that exists and defeated by a field anything can put anything in.** The rule must name a registered rule; the symbol and file must be non-empty; the file must be **repository-relative**, and that is not tidiness — `envelope.scan` moves anything path-shaped out of the hashed payload under FR-055, so an absolute path would carry the provenance out of the artifact's identity at wrap time; the content hash must be a lowercase `sha256:` address and is taken over **the source construct the rule read**, so FR-028 has something to compare; `analyzer_version` is ours and is deliberately a different constant from the vendored revision, since collapsing them would make one of our releases read as an upstream one
  - **`validated` is unconstructible without naming the artifact, and the artifact may not be the source the derivation read.** Constitution Principle I as amended at v1.1.0, held in `__post_init__` rather than in review. `PROVISIONAL` is the default and the derivation emits nothing else, because a static derivation holds no independent artifact — T122 is what may promote one
  - ⚠️ **One open item recorded rather than fixed, and it is a schema decision this task does not own.** `analyzer_version` sits under the derived artifact's content address and `schemas.py` does not declare it volatile, so **bumping the analyzer changes the address of every derived contract and check** and FR-028 reads that as source drift. Same false-alarm shape `served_operations.py` argues about for `schema_version` — *a release of ours is not the source clock ticking* — one level down. Named here and in the module so it is a known item rather than a surprise at the first version bump
  - ⚠️➡️✅ **`DERIVED_CONTRACT` v1.0.0 does not list `provenance` in `required`, while FR-026 requires it on every derived contract *and* every derived check.** So the producer satisfies FR-026 and the ~~**schema does not yet enforce it for any producer**~~ **schema enforced it for no producer until 2026-08-12**. ~~`DERIVED_CHECK` does require it.~~ **`DERIVED_CHECK` named it in `required`, which is a presence test over top-level keys, so the string `"signature"` satisfied it — the same gap one level down rather than the exception this sentence read as.** ~~Closing the gap is a schema bump with a migration and it belongs with **T133**'s coverage test; it was not done quietly here~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-12 by [OD-32](../001-discovery-validation/plan.md)**, and it was the schema bump with a migration this bullet predicted: both kinds move to 1.1.0 with FR-026's six fields required *as data*, behind a version gate that keeps a 1.0.0 artifact readable with its provenance explicitly absent and named rather than back-filled. It did not wait for **T133**, which still owes the coverage *count* over real derivations
  - **`EXPECTED_PROOFS` moves `360 → 374` with OD-32's fourteen arms, read off the guard's own transition message rather than computed from a baseline and a delta.** Written here after the fact because the pass that moved it was not permitted to edit this file, and **re-observed rather than transcribed**: planting `360` back over the constant returns *"the proof set moved from 360 to 374"* from `test_the_declared_proof_count_is_what_it_is_expected_to_be`, and `tools/check_tampers.py` then reads `374 proofs declared` with the guard passing, so the destination is current by construction as well as named by a firing. The form is the fired-guard transition the `163 → 164` and `182 → 192` entries above use and not the bare count at T101, because a firing named this one. **This is the third such note in this file and this file is not the constant's ledger** — the line has been written in **54** commits, and the record carrying every movement is the commit message, `927fd72` for this one
- [X] T122 [US2] Mark a derived contract `validated` when it agrees with the target's **published specification** and `provisional` with provenance and confidence otherwise, in `src/analysis/validate.py` (T-14, FR-026, constitution Principle I as amended at v1.1.0 — ~~the independent artifact is in hand for every admitted target because FR-002 makes it an admission criterion~~ **true of an admitted target and false of everything in this tree; see the fixture bullet**)
  - **Done 2026-08-12. `validated` or `provisional` with the reason named as a closed enum member, not a boolean.** `ProvisionalReason` has five members because *"nothing corroborated this"* has four genuinely different causes and the promotion bug reads three of them as agreement: `NO_SPECIFICATION`, `OPERATION_NOT_SERVED`, `SPECIFICATION_SILENT`, `SPECIFICATION_UNREADABLE`, `SPECIFICATION_DISAGREES`. An **explicitly empty** parameter list is deliberately *not* silence — it is a claim the operation takes none, and a derivation that read three disagrees with it. This is the **absent / provisional** pair `derived_record.py` holds apart, one level up
  - **The comparison is passed in and never fetched, and that is scanned rather than trusted.** FR-002 makes the published specification a **runtime-fetched artifact** — spec.md:758 is explicit that it is *"fetched from the running deployment"* — while requiring source analysis to stay *"reproducible from the codebase alone, with no network input"*. So it arrives as a `ServedOperationSet` from the admission stage above, and a test scans this module's imports for a network client, on the same reasoning `served_operations.py` scans its own
  - **The join is declared and never inferred.** A derived contract's identifier is `module:function` and a served operation's comes from the specification, so `served_operation_id` is a required argument. Guessing it is how *fluent, plausible and wrong* arrives — finding 007 measured 15 of 69 endpoints carrying a contract wrong about every field name on the wire with nothing in the output indicating it
  - ⚠️ **The schema ruling: no bump. 1.1.0 already carries this, and the task brief's premise that a validation status is a new field is wrong.** T121 put `validation_status` **inside** `provenance` and `FR_026_PROVENANCE_FIELDS` already requires it on both derived kinds, so promotion changes a field's **value** and adds no field. The conditional seventh, `validated_against`, was already in `to_payload` and is permitted because `_validate_provenance` checks the presence of the required six without rejecting additional keys. T121's precedent is **followed**: 1.1.0 moved because two different *shapes* would otherwise claim one version, and no shape moves now. The binding consideration — *a contract reading back with no validation status must not be indistinguishable from one that read back `validated`* — was already discharged three times over by T121 and OD-32 (the schema refuses a 1.1.0 provenance missing the field; `from_payload` refuses an absent or unknown status rather than defaulting it; an absent provenance *entirely* is `ProvenanceState.ABSENT`, a third value, and `require_provenance()` raises)
  - ⚠️ **Which fixture yields which status, and the question is malformed for one of them.** `inventory-service/` yields **three contracts, all `provisional`, reason `NO_SPECIFICATION`** — it is source on disk, there is no running deployment, therefore no published specification, therefore no independent artifact. `no-derivable-checks/` yields **no contracts at all and therefore no validation status**; an answer of `provisional` would be wrong in a way that matters, because it would mean a contract existed. **So the promotion path is exercised only by specification inputs constructed in tests and has never run against a real published specification.** A specification hand-written from the same `service.py` the derivation read would be an artifact its own derivation *did* produce, which is precisely what Principle I forbids relying on, so one was not committed
  - ⚠️ **`validated` is corroboration of the derivation and three things it is not**, each stated because each is a reading somebody will take. It is **not** a claim any response is correct — conformance to a declared shape is explicitly not verification (T124, FR-022). It is **not** verified correspondence: `correspondence.verified_correspondence()` always raises, v1 has no mechanism binding a commit to a running instance, so the association between the source symbol and the served operation rests on FR-057's operator **declaration**. What promotion does buy is exactly what FR-057 says — a source reference pointing at the **wrong application** surfaces here as contracts that fail to validate — and the residual FR-057 names, *the right repository at the wrong commit*, survives it. And it does **not promote the contract's checks**, which stay `provisional`: a specification agreeing about parameter names says nothing about whether an aggregate recomputation is correct, and promoting one on that evidence would be the derived-but-wrong verifier arriving through the mechanism built to catch it

  - ~~**PARTIAL 2026-08-12, and the unexercised half is named below. The construction is built and a type checker confirms it; the type checker is not in this repository's gate set.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-12 — the sole ground this PARTIAL stood on is satisfied, and the tick is clean** (T016's precedent for the form). INV-012
  - **Why it closed, and why a satisfied ground is spent rather than merely weakened.** The PARTIAL offered exactly one ground — *the type checker is not in this repository's gate set* — and that ground is now false: `ci.yml`'s `invariants` job carries a `type check` step running `python -m mypy` over the whole of `src/` against an error count declared in the workflow, `tools/instruments.py` registers it as a **gate**, and `requirements.lock` pins the checker so the two static arms **run** in CI instead of skipping. This is the shape [**finding 028**](./findings/028-od24-deferral-re-examination.md) ruled on for OD-24's second ground: *a ground that says wait until we know stops being a reason to wait when we know*, and the ground is **discharged by satisfaction rather than falsified** — the reason was real, it was collected, it is used up. A deferral whose only ground is spent has nothing left holding it
  - **What is *not* a surviving ground, stated because a later reader will reach for it.** Python has no sealed constructor, so a `ValidatedContract` can be hand-built — see the residual bullet below. That is **not** an unexercised half of this task and was never offered as one. This task's sentence is that a ***provisional*** contract never produces verified, and a hand-built `ValidatedContract` is not a `ProvisionalContract`; its mechanism is enforcement ***at the type level***, and an object of the correct type carrying false field values is a lie in data, which no type system refuses. Fabricated promotion is finding 007's defect class and answers to `Provenance.__post_init__` and to the promotion path in `validate_contract`, not to this barrier. A PARTIAL names work declined; *Python lacks sealed constructors* is a property of the language that no amount of work here removes, and ticking PARTIAL against it would make the tick permanently unclosable
  - **The runtime half of this sentence already existed and is not what this task added.** `src/contracts/result.py` has refused `provisional and VERIFIED` since T021, and its own comment defers the analysis boundary to T123. A runtime `if` satisfies the sentence's letter and not its point: a caller reaches it with the forbidden pair already in hand. So what is added is **unreachability** — `ProvisionalContract` and `ValidatedContract` are distinct types with no inheritance, `Verified` names `ValidatedContract` in its own constructor, and no method on `ProvisionalContract` returns `Verified`, asserted by introspection over the annotations so a method added later is covered without anyone updating a list

The arm asserts the error **names `issued_by`**, because an exit code alone is satisfied by a syntax error or an unresolved import

The mechanism is a partition, not a list of three names.** `VerificationOutcome` carries **four** members because `MODEL_ASSESSED` must stay distinct under Principle I, and `REPORTED_STATE` maps every outcome onto exactly one of `ReportedState`'s three. `Result.state` is what a consumer branches on, and `render_result` puts `state` on the wire beside `verification` — both, because collapsing them loses either the requirement's exhaustiveness or Principle I's distinction
  - **Exhaustiveness fails when a fourth arrives without being told.** A test listing three names is a change-detector an editor satisfies on both sides. The two directions are reported separately and deliberately not collapsed into a symmetric difference, because a name in both lists is the signature of a **rename** and is mechanically indistinguishable from one drop plus one add
  - **The one hole a partition cannot close is closed against the requirement itself.** Remapping an existing outcome onto a newly added fourth state keeps the map total, disjoint and complete, so every structural arm stays green. What it cannot keep is agreement with FR-025's numeral, so `test_the_state_count_agrees_with_the_requirement_that_declares_it` reads *"exactly one of N states"* out of `spec.md` — the `lifecycle-taxonomy` shape, which exists because the terminal taxonomy and `data-model.md` §2.1 diverged **in both directions at once** with nothing reading one against the other. A per-state arm covers the reverse: a state the requirement does not name fails
  - **T126's residual is not absorbed by the three, exactly as this task's note warned.** The unevidenced case is **unconstructible** rather than nameable: `VERIFIED` is refused alongside `PROVISIONAL` (T021's refusal, kept) and alongside `NOT_STATED` (new)

T123's type-level work makes `verified` unreachable from a `ProvisionalContract` in `src/analysis/validate.py`; nothing carries that into a `ResultRecord` a caller builds by hand. Either this task or T126 must make the unevidenced case unconstructible rather than merely nameable — and T128's staleness field is the precedent for the shape: a separate field, never a fourth state.

A bare flag satisfies this line and loses what bounds the risk — the ceiling is measured from the last successful fetch, so a stale marking that cannot say how old it is records the exposure without recording what closes it. `Staleness` carries all three and refuses a stale marking missing either

Three arms: the stage stops; what it says is *upstream schema change*, naming the pinned revision and the expected digest, because bumping the pin is the fix; and **no source-derived artifact is published**

What is checkable is the detector's *input*: FR-028 reads a changed content address on a `source_derived` artifact kind, `schemas.py` enumerates which those are, and the stage is driven through a real `ArtifactStore` with a real publish downstream of the index. On a mismatch every source-derived ref is unmoved. A separate arm pins the kind set so a new one cannot slip past, and **a positive control publishes for real**, without which every arm here would pass on a stage that refuses everything
  - **Its removal proof is deliberately *not* the pin-ordering tamper T119 uses.** That one would turn four tests red across two files and prove nothing about this file in particular. This proof moves only the **wording** — "upstream schema change, NOT source drift" becomes "Schema drift was detected here" — leaving `verify()` firing, the stage stopping and nothing published. Exactly one test goes red, and the failure it constructs is the real one: the analysis correctly refuses and the operator is sent to their own repository to look for a change they did not make
  - **Independence from T119 confirmed as T004 predicted it.** Setting the digest did not discharge this and the two tasks were built in one pass without either blocking the other

> **Expect a provisional share and do not read it as a fault.** Finding 007 measured this comparison
> on one target and one framework: the literal reading of its gate is **0.8696** and the validated
> reading **0.7681**, so roughly a quarter provisional on a comparable target is the expectation.
> That is one framework whose design premise is that the signature is the schema, and it must not be
> generalized — it is recorded so the share is expected rather than alarming.

**Checkpoint**: User Stories 1 and 2 both work independently.

## Phase 6: User Story 3 — Fail closed when the code or the deployment moves (Priority: P3)

**Goal**: two independently versioned clocks, drift detected separately on each, only the affected
operation disabled, and the whole thing loud rather than silent.

**Independent Test**: two synthetic corpora, each controlling its own change time. In the first,
mutate source so derived contracts no longer match, leaving the deployment untouched. In the second,
change what the deployment serves, leaving source untouched. Report detection, whether anything
unaffected was disabled, and how long detection took. **Plus the negative**: re-analysing unchanged
input produces no signal at all.

- [X] T137 [US3] Source-derived and deployment-derived artifacts as two **independently** versioned things, in `src/analysis/clocks.py` (FR-027, **OD-06** — a shared version cannot express that one changed and the other did not, which is the whole content of drift)
  - **Done 2026-08-13, and it defines no new hash function.** Both readings are composed from versions that already existed: the deployment clock's is T077's `set_version_of` over the canonical operation list, bound inside `deployment_reading` so the clock cannot be read off anything else; the source clock's are the content addresses of the source-derived kinds, which is the input `schemas.py` already declares FR-028 reads. What T137 owed was their **independence** and **separate detection**, and `compare_each` returns one movement per clock, each computed from that clock's own pair
  - **⚠️ `schemas.py`'s `source_derived` boolean is the *union* of the two clocks and not the source clock, and the flag's name says otherwise.** `served_operation_set` is flagged `source_derived=True` and it is the deployment-derived artifact — produced above source analysis, by the stage boundary `served_operations.py` draws in its first paragraph. The flag is not repaired by flipping a bit: its two consumers, `test_canonical_determinism.py` and `test_codegraph_schema_pin.py`, both read it as *drift reads this kind*, which is true of all three. What it cannot say is **which** clock reads it. `KINDS_ON_CLOCK` is that partition and `assert_partition_total` ties it to the registry in both directions, so a ninth drift-relevant kind cannot appear unassigned and an assigned kind cannot be one no drift channel reads
  - **Independence is a refusal, not a convention.** `reading()` refuses a version for a kind that is not on the clock being read, so a source reading **cannot** be constructed carrying the served surface. Without that branch the two clocks report movement together on a deployment change, which is OD-06's fused artifact reassembled one layer up. It refuses the opposite direction too: a clock read over a subset of its own kinds is silent for every change confined to the kinds it skipped
  - **The anchor is beside the version and not inside it, symmetric with T077 keeping the deployment identity out of `set_version`.** FR-057's declared reference is required on a source reading — `correspondence.py` already states that a source clock with no anchor is not a clock — and is refused on a deployment reading, which would be the two clocks back in one field. Hashing it in would move the source clock for a commit that invalidated no derived contract, and would let an operator move it by **re-declaring configuration**
  - **⚠️ One residual, named rather than closed: a schema release of ours moves the source clock.** The source-derived kinds' content addresses cover `schema_version`. `served_operations.py` closes exactly this for the *deployment* clock by making `set_version` a function of the served surface, and says why — *a schema release of ours is not the deployment clock ticking*. The symmetric repair is a `set_version` equivalent on `derived_contract` and `derived_check`, which is a change to **those artifacts** at a new schema version and not an input this module could choose differently. The false alarm is visible and operator-actionable rather than silent
  - **Built so that T139 and T140 are not forced into a rewrite.** FR-031 is narrowed by FR-047: a failed re-fetch has no *after* artifact version because no artifact was obtained, so the drift signal is a sum type. A `Reading` is therefore a complete value naming no successor, `compare` is a module function rather than a method, and `compare_each` is documented as the both-artifacts-obtained case that T140's shape does not pass through
  - **Sixteen removal proofs, every one established by planting rather than by reasoning.** The first sweep scored the subset refusal UNPROVEN, and the cause was the harness's own `drop_bytecode` being absent from the throwaway planting script: two tampers of equal length written inside one second share a cached `.pyc`, so the second arm ran the first arm's module. That is the defect class the harness already guards; the check that found it was the check being run for real instead of assumed
- [X] T138 [US3] Source-drift detection in the **same automated check run** as the change that caused it, in `src/analysis/source_drift.py` (FR-028, **SC-008**)
  - **Done 2026-08-13, and it defines no new comparison.** T137's `compare_each` already answers which clock moved; a second `version_before != version_after` here could disagree with it. The source-clock slice is `Movement.clock == SOURCE`, turned into an `ArtifactDrift` through `signals_from_movements` / `from_movement` — T139's only constructor. The finding is not a third `DriftSignal` variant
  - **Invalidation is not "the document hashed differently".** C-005, C-006 and C-007 all move the contract document's content address and none of them invalidate a caller. The breaking verdict and the drifted-operation list are derived from the atomic diff in this module, and T154's loader now *imports* those functions rather than restating them, so a second classifier cannot silently disagree with the instrument SC-008 is measured on. SC-008 is scored against that corpus in `tests/contract/test_source_drift.py`, one `detect()` call per revision, honouring the run↔revision bijection the loader already enforces
  - **⚠️ `schemas.py`'s `source_derived` boolean is the union of both clocks, consumed as T137 named it rather than flipped.** Filtering on the flag would report a deployment-clock move as source drift because `served_operation_set` is flagged `source_derived=True`. The partition remains `KINDS_ON_CLOCK`. A `codegraph` schema-hash mismatch is T136's analysis-stage refusal and never reaches this detector — this module does not import the pin

  - **Six removal proofs plus T154's rename-signature arm retargeted onto this module**, because the classifier moved with the detector. `EXPECTED_PROOFS` moves **493 → 499**, read off the guard's own transition message rather than computed from a baseline and a delta, and moved at both coupled sites including the illustration in its comment block
- [X] T139 [US3] Drift signal record stating which clock moved, the artifact versions before and after, and the deployment identity, in `src/analysis/drift_signal.py` (FR-031)
- [X] T140 [US3] The failed-re-fetch signal shape, where there is no *after* artifact version: the specification state found, named from FR-044's four-state classification, plus the timestamp of the last successful fetch, in `src/analysis/drift_signal.py` (FR-031's narrowing, FR-047)
  - **Done 2026-08-13, both rows in one pass, because they are two shapes of one record and building T139 first with a required before/after pair would have forced a rewrite of it and every consumer when T140 landed.** T137's note above anticipated exactly this and the anticipation held: `Reading` was already a complete value naming no successor and `compare` was already a module function, so nothing in `clocks.py` had to move
  - **The record is a sum over two shapes — `ArtifactDrift | FailedRefetch` — and not a product with `version_after: str | None`.** A `None` in that field has two readings no consumer can separate: *no artifact was obtained*, which is FR-047's fact about the world, and *nobody filled it in*, a fact about the code path. That collapse is this repository's recorded worst defect class — the wall-clock numerator was a missing quantity indistinguishable from a measured zero, and `spend_usd: float | None` exists to hold UNPRICED apart from COST NOTHING. `FailedRefetch` therefore **has no `version_after` attribute at all**: reading one is an `AttributeError` and a `mypy` error rather than a value that flows onward, and the serialized document carries no such key either. Absence is the mechanism, because a validator saying *"this field must be None on this variant"* is the product type with a removable rule bolted on
  - **T139 consumes T137's comparison rather than restating it.** `ArtifactDrift.from_movement` is the only constructor and takes a `Movement`; `compare_each` already answers FR-031's *which of the two clocks moved*, and a second comparison here could disagree with the first. `signals_from_movements` is the filter that carries the phase's Independent Test — *re-analysing unchanged input produces no signal at all* — because `compare_each` returns one movement per clock whether or not it moved
  - **⚠️ The specification-state vocabulary is five members where FR-047's own sentence says three, and the committed corpus is what decided it — not a preference.** FR-031's narrowing says the state is *"named from FR-044's four-state classification"* and FR-047 says *"any of FR-044's three non-admissible states"*. But `tests/fixtures/spec-withdrawn/corpus.json`'s `withdraw-past-ceiling` declares two fetches in state `unreachable`, which is one of the two states `src/analysis/admission.py` declares **beyond** FR-044's list, and its calls carry `specification_state_last_found: "unreachable"`. A three-member domain could not represent a scenario this repository committed at T157. The domain is therefore the classifier's states **minus the admissible one**, computed as a subtraction from `admission.py` rather than written out. `published_non_empty` is refused, and that refusal is load-bearing: a successful fetch recorded in the shape meaning *no artifact was obtained* makes the two arms of the sum overlap, which is the same collapse the optional field would cause arriving through the value domain instead of the type
  - **The residual that leaves open, named rather than closed.** Whether a target going `unreachable` should enter the stale state on the same rule as one going `absent` is **T147's** disposition. This module records the state that was found and refuses only the one that would make the record false; it does not widen FR-047's trigger set, and `test_the_corpus_reaches_a_state_outside_fr_044s_four` pins the corpus fact the widening rests on, so the day the corpus stops exercising `unreachable` the justification fails loudly instead of persisting unsupported
  - **The drift signal and FR-047's caller-visible marking share a vocabulary and deliberately do not share a type, because the second fact is not the same fact.** FR-047 asks `Staleness` for *"the age of that set"*; FR-031's narrowing asks this record for *"the timestamp of the last successful fetch"*. Those are an interval and an instant, and the instant is the primitive — age is `now - timestamp` and storing it would store a number wrong immediately after it is written. `FailedRefetch.age_seconds(now)` derives it on `ServedOperationSet.age_seconds`'s no-default-clock signature, so the two records cannot state two different ages. The one fact they do share is the state, and `test_the_state_vocabulary_agrees_with_the_caller_visible_marking` extends to a third site the check `result.py` already declares for its duplicated `SPECIFICATION_STATES` — *"a test asserts the two agree, which is a check, where an import would be an assumption"*. **T148's caller-visible marking is not built here**
  - **The corpus was used as a falsification test and it falsified a candidate, which is why it is committed as one.** `test_every_spec_withdrawn_scenario_is_representable` builds a `FailedRefetch` for all nine non-published fetches across the five scenarios, and `test_the_derived_age_matches_what_the_corpus_declares` recomputes FR-047's age from the instant this record holds and asserts it against the `age_seconds` T157 wrote down independently — seven stale calls, all agreeing. A record storing an age instead of the instant would leave that second arm with nothing to check
  - **Fourteen removal proofs, every one established by planting rather than by reasoning**, mirroring the harness's `drop_bytecode` on the way in and on both ways out. Eleven remove a guard; the last three run the other way and **add the product type back** — at the type, at the serialized document, and at the discriminant — because "sum, not product" is a claim that needs a counterfactual rather than a docstring. `EXPECTED_PROOFS` moves **479 → 493**, read off the guard's own transition message rather than computed from a baseline and a delta, and moved at both coupled sites including the illustration in its comment block

> **The module paths in T141 through T146 were moved 2026-08-13 out of `src/runtime/drift_*.py` and
> into `src/runtime/drift/`, and the package is load-bearing rather than tidy — do not simplify them
> back to flat modules.** `tests/invariants/test_sandbox_reachability.py` declares `SANDBOX_ROOTS` as
> two **directories**, `src/sandbox/` and `src/runtime/drift/`, so INV-003 reaches these modules by
> path prefix. A module built at `src/runtime/drift_scheduler.py` is a *sibling* of that directory
> rather than a member of it, and would therefore sit outside the only check in the tree that catches
> a literal hostname or URL appearing in sandbox-side source — the regression that check was written
> for, and under **OD-12** exactly how the scheduler's re-fetch becomes a second continuous path to
> the target, which is what T142 exists to prevent.
>
> **Nor is the gap to be repaired from the other end by adding the flat filenames to
> `SANDBOX_ROOTS`.** A directory scan is total over every module that will ever land in it, where a
> file list covers only the names someone thought of on the day they wrote it; being total is why
> `src/runtime/drift/__init__.py` was created empty and early, as its own docstring records.
> **Nothing these rows ask for became wrong**, so this is a path substitution and not a strike: the

## Phase 7: User Story 4 — Run it in your own environment, on your own model provider (Priority: P4)

**Goal**: every component inside the operator's boundary, bring-your-own-credentials across at least
four providers, and no secret anywhere the model or the environment can read.

**Independent Test**: complete the User Story 1 battery once per supported provider with
configuration as the only difference, and scan every trace, artifact and persisted record — and the
execution environment itself — asserting no secret value appears.

**Dependency worth stating.** This story *constrains* the first three more than it delivers a
separate slice, which is why the provider driver sits in Phase 3 and only the four-provider battery
and the credential planes sit here.

- [ ] T159 [US4] Four OCI images — analysis, runtime, supervisor, enforcement point — plus the sandbox base image, in `deploy/images/` (T-11, **OD-08**)
  - **The supervisor image is where a second writer on the session store first becomes possible, and nothing enforces the condition that currently keeps it safe. Recorded 2026-08-06 against [T016](#storage-addressing-and-rollback).** [Finding 033](./findings/033-session-table-wal-race-unreachable-and-owed-to-migration.md) measured `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` being refused with `SQLITE_BUSY` **four orders of magnitude inside the busy timeout** when a second party first-opens a *brand-new* store, because SQLite deliberately bypasses the busy retry on the conversion path. `SessionTable.__init__` runs that pragma as its schema script's **first** statement, so a refused conversion aborts before `CREATE TABLE` and leaves the file with no `session` table in it at all. A store already in WAL is immune — a second open succeeds in 0.28 ms even against an `EXCLUSIVE` holder — so the hazard is specifically **concurrency on the conversion**, which happens once in a file's life. The property this deployment must preserve, stated so its loss is checkable: **the session store is created once, before any second process attaches**, *or* `session_table.py` has completed the migration T016 records. Neither is asserted anywhere today, and the supervisor image plus T160's compose bundle is the first artifact that can falsify the first one.
- [ ] T160 [US4] The compose bundle we author, with reference-application values marked under FR-043 as fixture configuration rather than product defaults, in `deploy/compose/` (T-11)
  - **Extended 2026-08-04 by [finding 024](./findings/024-deployment-surface-permission-census.md): the bundle carries two runtime-permission artifacts, and without them the three kernel facilities T006 checks for are present and unreachable.** **(a) Its own seccomp profile**, in `deploy/compose/seccomp/session.json` — the container runtime's own default plus one added `SCMP_ACT_ALLOW` rule naming `unshare`, `mount`, `umount2`, `pivot_root`, `setns`, `mount_setattr`, `move_mount` and `open_tree`, with the argument mask removed from the non-`CAP_SYS_ADMIN` `clone` rule so namespace flags pass; 426 allow-listed syscall names becoming 427. Referenced from the compose file by `security_opt`, so the operator's action is a file we ship rather than a flag they compose. **(b) `/sys/fs/cgroup` mounted read-write with `--cgroupns=host`**, which is a separate change refused by a separate layer — the read-only mount, not seccomp — and is not fixed by any profile decision
  - **Two things this task may not do.** It may **not** offer `seccomp=unconfined` as an alternative or present the choice as default-profile-versus-unconfined: that framing charges the operator the whole filter for a mechanism a named eight-syscall widening buys, and `unconfined` leaves FR-049 exactly as blocked as it was. And it may not describe the cgroup mount's cost as narrower than it is — the supervisor container gets write access to the host's entire cgroup tree rather than to a delegated subtree, and no route to narrowing that has been found (FR-049, **FR-043**)
- [ ] T161 [US4] Two credential planes: the provider credential held by the runtime, the target credential held by the enforcement point and injected on re-origination, neither reaching the execution environment, in `src/contracts/credentials.py` (FR-036, FR-050)
- [ ] T162 [US4] The analysis/runtime/target boundary explicit in configuration even when all three run on one host, with co-location never assumed, in `src/contracts/topology.py` (FR-034)
- [ ] T163 [US4] Provider selection by configuration with **no provider-specific behaviour in the core path**, in `src/runtime/providers/select.py` (FR-037)
- [ ] T164 [US4] Run the full User Story 1 battery against **at least four independent providers** with configuration as the only difference between runs, in `tests/batteries/test_four_providers.py` (**SC-010** — now a test v1 must pass rather than a result it inherits)
- [ ] T165 [P] [US4] Automated secret scan over model context, emitted artifacts, traces and persisted state, running on **every** session, in `tests/batteries/test_secret_scan.py` (FR-036, **SC-004**)
- [ ] T166 [P] [US4] In-container scan asserting no secret is readable from the environment, the process table, or any mount in the declared set, in `tests/batteries/test_in_container_scan.py` (FR-050's not-present clause, **SC-024**)
- [ ] T167 [P] [US4] Not-inherited test: nothing written inside one session's environment is readable from a later session's, in `tests/batteries/test_environment_not_inherited.py` (FR-050, SC-024)
- [ ] T168 [P] [US4] Assert no operator-specific path, hostname, address or credential is written into any emitted artifact, in `tests/contract/test_artifact_portability.py` (FR-033)
- [ ] T169 [US4] Operator-boundary check: every component runs inside the operator's boundary and no target data or credential is required to leave it, in `tests/integration/test_operator_boundary.py` (FR-032)
- [ ] T170 [P] [US4] Cassette-backed provider tests over the core path, in `tests/conformance/` (constitution Principle VII, added because the specification does not capture it for v1)
- [ ] T171 [US4] Exercise the fail-loud startup path end to end through the shipped bundle, in `tests/integration/test_bundle_failloud.py` (FR-033)
  - ~~**This task presupposes a startup the Python components do not have, measured 2026-08-06 and recorded at [the configuration section](#configuration-and-failing-closed).**~~ **Unblocked 2026-08-08 by T211**, which built it. `config.load()` ~~has no caller in `src/`, and neither does OD-27's `require_priceable` preflight~~ **is called by `src/supervisor/main.py` and `src/runtime/main.py`, and `require_priceable` by the second of the two**; the twelve no-default keys under four authorities are reachable and their reason text is asserted by content in `tests/contract/test_startup_entry_points.py`
  - **What is still owed here is the half this task actually names, and it is narrower than it was.** T211 exercises the path **in-process**; this exercises it **through the shipped bundle**, which is a different claim — that the images T159 builds run these modules, with the environment T160's compose file supplies, and refuse in a container the same way they refuse on a developer's host. The commands to write it against are `python -m src.supervisor.main` and `python -m src.runtime.main`, and the assertion to carry across is the content one: an exit status alone cannot tell an unset ceiling from a missing interpreter, and a container makes the second more likely rather than less
- [ ] T172 [P] [US4] Assert every supported-platform surface states **Linux only with no degraded mode**, in `tests/contract/test_platform_statement.py` (**OD-17**, FR-053, SC-027 — a degraded mode is a sandbox missing one of Principle IV bullet 1's terms)

**Checkpoint**: the product is installable and portable across providers inside an operator's own
boundary.

## Phase 8: User Story 5 — Learn from production whether the three claims hold (Priority: P5)

**Goal**: the instrumentation, not just the capabilities. All three of v1's differentiating claims
ship unmeasured, so this phase builds what would measure them.

**Independent Test**: over a fixed window, produce three reports — the verifier's marginal detection
over the shadow judge with the pre-registered gate applied and all three branches intact; the effect
gate's read-only precision against a labelled corpus of real operations; and drift detection rate,
false-alarm rate and latency on each clock.

### The verifier's margin over a shadow judge

- [ ] T173 [US5] Shadow judge consuming the trace stream **asynchronously**, never in the request path, writing `judge_verdict` rows keyed to a result in a table nothing on the success path reads, in `src/runtime/judge/shadow.py` (FR-039)
- [ ] T174 [US5] Make the judge injectable so the same sessions can run with it agreeing, disagreeing, and not running at all, in `src/runtime/judge/inject.py` (**SC-025**)
- [ ] T175 [P] [US5] Differential battery asserting 100% of caller-visible result records and 100% of gate decisions are identical across the three runs, with zero behavioural differences attributable to the judge, in `tests/batteries/test_judge_differential.py` (**SC-025**, FR-052 — T025's import-graph test is the structural half of the same guarantee)
- [ ] T176 [US5] **Adjudication queue**: a sampling rule pre-registered **before** the window opens, an operator-facing surface presenting a sampled result with the evidence needed to judge it, and `human_label` rows carrying the adjudicator and the time, in `src/runtime/adjudication/` — **FR-040**'s third branch reads the judge's own discrimination, which needs human ground truth; the verifier's verdict cannot supply it without circularity and a model is the exact substitution **FR-052** exists to prevent
- [ ] T177 [US5] **FR-040** gate report with all three branches intact — ten percentage points or more makes the verifier a headline capability, a smaller margin makes it an internal detail, and a judge no better than chance triggers a constitutional prohibition independently of the verifier's score — carrying the stated precondition that **SC-013**'s window opens only once labelling capacity exists, in `src/runtime/reports/margin.py` ([`plan.md`](./plan.md) Complexity Tracking row 1; the corpus records that the one adjudication pass this needed was never performed and that a model stood in)

### The effect gate's read-only precision

- [ ] T178 [US5] Record per request at the enforcement point: the resolved tier, the rule identifier, the matched operation template, the method, the specification metadata that operation carried, and the disposition, in `src/proxy/observation.go` (FR-041 — that record *is* the corpus)
- [ ] T179 [US5] Corpus exporter producing the labelled set FR-041 scores against, in `src/runtime/reports/effect_corpus.py`
- [ ] T180 [US5] State-diff oracle on the reference application — snapshot the application's state, issue the call, diff — labelling read-only precision by **observable state** and not by any model judgement, in `tests/batteries/effect_gate_oracle.py` (FR-041, constitution Principle I's admissible artifacts)
- [ ] T181 [US5] Record the per-call threshold as **unset** and block every write capability until a threshold is pre-registered *for a per-call gate* and measured, in `src/runtime/reports/effect_precision.py` (FR-041, **SC-014**, **OD-10** — the superseded per-tool number does not travel: different base rate, different blast radius, and inventing one here is the inherited-number failure arriving by a new door)

### Drift detection on both clocks

- [ ] T182 [US5] Drift measurement harness reporting detection rate, false-alarm rate and detection latency **per clock**, in `tests/batteries/test_drift_measurement.py` (FR-042, **SC-015**)
- [ ] T183 [US5] Pre-register the measurement design **before the measurement runs**, naming which population each latency figure is measured on, in `docs/preregistration/drift.md` (FR-042)
- [ ] T184 [US5] Record that deployment-clock latency is measurable on the synthetic corpus because the corpus controls the change time, and generally **not** on real traffic unless the customer emits a deployment event FR-046 says may not be assumed, in `docs/preregistration/drift.md` — a property of the world, not a gap in the design, and inferring the change time from first observation would measure the detector against itself

### The measurement substrate all three depend on

- [ ] T185 [US5] `BatteryRun` freeze carrying **U-47**'s four terms — the prompt and request text **inside** the trace record, the battery version and task-file hashes pinned in the freeze, the cross-battery census pinned as an invariant re-checked on load, and an analysis path that **refuses** a cross-battery join — in `src/runtime/batteries/freeze.py` (FR-053)
- [ ] T186 [P] [US5] Four loader-refusal tests, one per U-47 term: an edited prompt, a changed battery version, a census mismatch, and an attempted cross-battery join, each **failing rather than warning**, in `tests/contract/test_battery_loader.py` — U-47 is the register entry recording a hash-pinned trace corpus that rebased onto edited prompts while every hash check kept passing
- [ ] T187 [P] [US5] Assert the measurement tables are structurally apart: no success-path table references `judge_verdict`, `human_label`, `effect_gate_observation` or `battery_run`, and no success-path module imports their writers, in `tests/invariants/test_measurement_isolation.py`
- [ ] T188 [US5] Define FR-045's reporting window as configuration and mark it unvalidated, in `src/runtime/reports/windows.py` — **the specification defines no window; see [Loose requirements](#loose-requirements-reported-not-worked-around) item 5**
- [ ] T189 [P] [US5] Audit every external product surface for the four prohibited claim shapes — capability advantage for an application-specific tool surface, synthesis being safer, a cost figure without basis and scope, and "provably" for effect resolution — in `docs/claims-audit.md` (FR-043, **SC-016**)
- [ ] T190 [P] [US5] Audit every statement of what the product supports for a language, framework or target shape with no committed fixture and asserted expected output, in `docs/support-audit.md` (FR-053, **SC-027**)
- [ ] T191 [US5] Measure, per deployed runtime, whether it is still serving traffic four weeks after installation, recording an installed-demonstrated-then-unused runtime as a **non-adoption** rather than an install, in `src/runtime/reports/adoption.py` (**SC-017**)
- [ ] T192 [US5] Standing report of every value still marked unvalidated — FR-046's detection window, FR-047's staleness ceiling, FR-049's two bounds, the lease interval — in `src/runtime/reports/unvalidated.py` (FR-043)
  - **Extended 2026-08-03**: the report also carries **the Linux kernel floor of 5.14 as DERIVED and NOT TESTED**, listed as a distinct kind rather than folded in with the four. The four are values an operator configures; the floor is a preflight constant read out of documented feature introduction rather than out of a boot, and it is the only entry a measurement would close (T205, **deferred by owner decision 2026-08-03**, so this entry stays on the report indefinitely rather than until a scheduled matrix run clears it). Whatever wording the report uses, it may not be weaker than the preflight's own, which states the derivation and the untested status together and has a removal proof behind it (**OD-17**, FR-053, FR-043)
- [ ] T205 [US5] **DEFERRED BY OWNER DECISION 2026-08-03 — not planned work for v1.** **Boot the supported-kernel matrix and convert the derived floor into a tested one** — 5.14, 5.15 LTS, 6.1 LTS and 6.6 LTS, running the FR-048, FR-049 and FR-050 mechanism batteries on each — in `.github/workflows/kernel-matrix.yml` (**OD-17**, FR-053). Until this exists, **every run to date was on 6.12 or 6.17** — `6.12.76-linuxkit` locally and `6.17.0-1020-azure` on the `ubuntu-latest` runner, the latter first observed 2026-08-04 when CI ran for the first time — and 5.14 is a lower bound on what *could* work rather than a statement that it does. Recording the boots is the whole task: cgroup delegation semantics, `pivot_root` in a user namespace and `seccomp` notification behaviour all moved across the intervening releases, so a green run on either of those kernels is evidence about that kernel and about nothing below it
  - **DEFERRED, NOT DROPPED, AND THE CAVEAT IS NOT RELAXED IN EXCHANGE** — the owner accepted shipping the derived floor **marked NOT TESTED** rather than building the matrix now, so this task is a recorded non-decision to measure and is not work anybody is waiting on. The task keeps its number and its full description because deferring the measurement is what makes the marking load-bearing: **5.14 remains DERIVED and NOT TESTED wherever it appears**, the preflight goes on stating the derivation and the untested status in one string, and the removal proof that fires when that caveat is dropped stays in the suite. Reinstated by an owner decision to measure, and by nothing else — in particular, not by a green run on any single kernel

**Checkpoint**: every claim the product makes either traces to a measurement or is marked
unvalidated, and the instruments that would close the three unmeasured ones exist.
